LLM Explains: The article describes the discovery of a murdered man's

Index Number: preciv447

Article:
Richmond , Oct . 1 - Within the last three days , we
understand that the dead body of a black man was floating
on the surface of the basin and was supposed to have risen
from its bottom , or that of the canal . It exhibited the
most suspicious signs of murder , the skull particularly
being very much battered and broken . It is said that the
deceased was a boatman from the upper country and that , in
an affray with another boatman , the deceased was knocked
in the head , dragged into the water and sunk . A coroner's
inquest called upon the body . It is said that the
principal , if not the only witness , is a deaf and dumb
woman , who made signs that she saw the murder at night and
then saw the body cast into the water . She is understood
to have made signs also that she will be able to recognize
the murderer . We understand that an order has been sent up
to Lynchburg for the purpose of apprehending the black man
suspected of the murder . The case of a murder being tried
on the evidence of a deaf and dumb person is perhaps a
singular one in the annals of jurisprudence . Who is to be
the interpreter in such a case ? What rule is to be pursued
?

field of rutabaga , which was overrun with grasshoppers ,
eating off the leaves and threatening the entire
destruction of the crop . He procured a drove of turkeys
and turned them into the lot and they made mincemeat of the
grasshoppers . The rutabagas were saved and the turkeys in

course in Kentucky on the subject of abolition , we are not

on Monday morning , it being the first day of the Circuit

, and the criminals were whipped promiscuously .

Headline:

LLM Explains: The article describes an actual event where mobs intended

rope was put around his neck, which constitutes an

Index Number: preciv440

Article:
An anti-slavery meeting was lately called in Boston , when
several thousand persons attended with the intention of
lynching Thompson and Garrison . The former did not make
his appearance , but they caught the latter , crouched
under a pile of boards in the second story of a carpenter's
shop . A rope was immediately fastened around his neck and
arms , and he was led into the streets , where the civil
officers arrested him and committed him to jail . He has
since been released and fed from the city . Thompson has
gone to Canada .

Seldom have we heard a more sickening detail of fanaticism
than that which was communicated to us a day or two since .
It appears that in the vicinity of Auburn there is a knave
by the name of Sweets who has gathered around him half a
score of silly women , who believe him to possess
extraordinary powers , and bound to obey him under all
circumstances at all times . To prove their obedience to
him was their duty , they are on one occasion ordered by
him to take a carving knife , and proceed through the
street and slay whatever might impede his progress . She
entered upon her task and had it not been the instrument of
death wrested from her hand by the first person she
attacked , she would have obeyed his command to the letter
. On another occasion , he commanded three of the women to
proceed on a Sabbath day to a neighboring church where
communion was about to be administered , to upset the table
, scatter the vessels and drink up the wine . Accordingly ,
at the proper hour , they entered the house singing and
dancing as they proceeded and performed the duty assigned
them . These outrages incensed the neighborhood , and it
was secretly determined by a few who had imbibed an
attachment to the code of Judge Lynch , to present the
whole household with a coat of tar and feathers . For this
purpose , some ten or twelve young men blackened their
faces and otherwise disguised themselves , and proceeded at
a late hour on Monday or Tuesday night of last week , to
Sweet's house , which they entered , seized several of the
inmates , ( among whom was Sweet ) covered their bodies
with tar , and then feathered them with the contents of a
bed which they took from the house for that purpose . The
intruders then retired but again soon returned and stole a
large quantity of maple sugar , with which they decamped .
On making their second visit , one of the disguised
gentlemen was recognized and the next day arrested ,
examined , and bound over for trial . During the
examination , the females repeatedly declared their belief
in the divine character of ' their lord , ' as they called
him , and their obligation to obey him implicitly . We have
not heard the result of the subsequent examination , nor
whether any disposition has been made of the impostor who
has been instrumental in establishing this new creed in
Cayuga . - Rochester Democrat .

Headline: 
Newspaper: The North-Carolina standard.
Date: 1837-11-08
Article ID: 23_1837-11-08_p4_sn85042147_00202197954_1837110801_0635
LLM Explains: The article describes a lynching event that has already
occurred, where the plaintiff was seized, tried, convicted,
and punished by a self-constituted 'Lynch Court.' Keywords
like 'Lynch Court,' 'seized,' 'convicted,' and 'punishment'
support this categorization.
Index Number: preciv436

Article:
Lynch Law . - The Nashville Banner , of the 30th ult . ,
contains a full report of a trial in the Circuit Court of
the United States for the District of West Tennessee , in
which Arson Moony was plaintiff , and Egbert Shepherd ,
Joseph Jones , Jacob F . Harrington , and 10 others ,
citizens of the State , were defendants . The transaction
out of which it grew took place on the 4th of September ,
1835 . The plaintiff , it seems , had fallen under
suspicion as a kidnapper , or slave stealer , whereupon
certain inhabitants of Brownsville constituted themselves a
Lynch Court , seized the plaintiff in the dead of night ,
tried him , convicted him , and then proceeded to
punishment by inflicting one hundred lashes with cowskin ,
branding him on the cheek with the letter R , and
commanding him to leave the country . The trial was
regularly conducted , defendants ' witnesses being
subpoenaed and brought into court , some of them from a
distance of forty miles , at the expense of the now
defendants . It was very numerously attended by spectators
and assistants from all parts of the county . During the
progress of the trial , some merciful individuals among the
crowd suggested the expediency of hanging the now plaintiff
, but their advice was overruled by a solemn vote . The
jury in the Circuit Court gave a verdict of 1000 dollars
and costs against five of the defendants .

With very rapid step , thus led , white cloth being thrown

. Milledgeville , Nov . 16 , 1837 , A novel and interesting
incident occurred in the House of Representatives this
morning . One of the Bibb members presented a memorial from
Robert Beasley , stating that some few years ago he
purchased of a slave trader a negro woman and her sucking
child not doubting but that the woman was its mother .
Subsequent developments , however , had convinced him that
an imposition had been practiced upon him in the sale of
the child that instead of being born of a slave , it was
the offspring of a white woman from whom it had been stolen
, or who had abandoned it . The object of the memorial was
, to move the Legislature to change the name of the
foundling from that of William Gas ( known in the bill of
sale of the slave trader ) to that of William Crockett
Beasley and to restore to him the natural rights of which
he had been unjustly deprived . A thrill of admiration
passed through the House . Mr . Beasley was in the lobby
and held up the child in his arms a fairer , prettier ,
more intelligent looking boy , need never be desired . Many
an old bachelor's mouth watered . The House went into
committee of the whole , had the child in the midst of them
, and everyone we believe was satisfied of his whiteness
and his title to his freedom . Macon Telegraph . Lynch Law
- On Tuesday evening the 25th ult . it was reported at the
town of Warren , Ky . that an individual who lives about a
mile from that town , had whipped his wife most
unmercifully , and that she was not expected to live . This
of course , raised considerable excitement among the
citizens , and some time after dark , a number of persons
went and brought the individual to town . A sham trial was
held over him , Judge Lynch presiding , and the unfortunate
prisoner was sentenced to be stripped , tied to a post ,
and to receive one hundred lashes with a rawhide which
sentence was immediately put into execution . We understand
that communication has been addressed by the Secretary of
State of the United States , by direction of the President
, to the Governor of this State , requesting his attention
to any movements , growing out of the present contest in
the Canadas , that may violate the laws of the United
States , passed to preserve the relations of amity with
foreign powers and fulfill the obligations of our treaties
with them , and requesting also his prompt interference to
arrest the parties concerned , if any preparations are made
of a hostile nature against any foreign power in amity with
the United States . Similar communications have been
addressed to the Executives of Vermont and Michigan , and
to the District Attorneys and marshals of those States and

the lake , retired to his berth . The lights of Cleveland ,
my port of destination soon appeared in view . I selected
my portmanteau from the general mass of luggage ; and ,
while inquiring for a porter , I saw a police officer
busily engaged in handcuffing the psalm singer . The
constable had been some time on the watch for his victim ,
who was principal agent for the western gang of
counterfeiters .

He is the thief and will soon confess ! ' The lynchers

A Fatal Attempt at Lynching . Vicksburg has been the scene

family of four Germans , named Flekenstein , ( a father and
three sons , ) who lived in the upper part of the town ,

demanded admission . This being refused , they broke open
the outer door and entered , J . A . Helt , a German , and
an unusually rash and adventurous young fop , leading the
way . Before he entered he called to his comrades ' who
will go in with me if I lead the way ? ' Several replied
that they would . The family was in the upper story of the
house . He commenced ascending the stairs , when , perhaps
dreading the resistance of some one he saw , or more
effectively to clear the way , he discharged one of his

Article:
a game played upon him by the deck hands and firemen on
board one of our Western steamers , a game even stronger
than that played by Second Municipality on this class of
the community in New Orleans . It seems that he had made
out to strike up a small game of poker with some of the

some trick , which at once led the honest steamboatmen into
the secret of how the thing was done and proved that they
lost their money by any other than the ' clean thing . '
The game , as a matter of course , was blocked at once ,
and demonstration immediately made that the gambler should
fork over his ill-gotten gains . This he flatly refused to
do ; said that he had won the money fair , and that he was
very clear of parting with what he had come honestly by .
They still persisted , and he still refused . The boat then
stopped to wood , when the men , finding it useless to
attempt regaining their money by fair means , resorted to a
plan which the gambler undoubtedly thought foul . Having
gained the consent of the engineer to use the engine , they
concocted a plan rather bordering on that code of laws
generally known as coming under the especial jurisdiction
of Judge Lynch . They in the first place made one end of a
rope fast to the neck of the wondering gambler , while the
other was tied to the end of the piston rod , allowing him
two or three feet slack . They told him that unless he
shelled out their money instantly , they would work the
engine , and at the same time that they were not
responsible for any injuries he might sustain . Loth to
give up his gains , the fellow cast one look at the new
system of extortion , coolly calculated his chances , and
then told them they might ‘ work away and be d—d . ' No
sooner said than done , and the gambler was immediately
seen chasing the piston rod upon all fours , and then
backing out of its way . His eye all the time was as firmly
set on the rod as ever that of Herr Cline or Gabriel Ravel
was upon the tight rope . After working forward and
backward several times , one of his tormentors asked him :
' Don't you think it best to hand over ? ' ' Don't bother
me , ' retorted the gambler . ' You'll get sick of that fun
, ' said another of the boatmen , as he was following the
piston rod up in the attitude of a bear . ' Not as you know
on , ' rejoined the gambler as he backed out of the way .
In this way they ran upon the poor fellow for some time ,
he still manifesting an unwillingness to give up his spoils
. By this time all the cabin passengers had heard of the
fun going on below , and went down to witness

Article:
LYNCHING . - On the 16th inst , a mob of several hundred
men took from the jail of Williamstown ( Ky . , ) two
persons Mays and Cook , suspected of the murder of a drover
named Uuerback . They then erected a scaffold on the spot
where the crime was committed , called two priests to
shrive the prisoners and hanged them up according to the
very letter of lynch law . The affair was creating a great
deal of excitement in Kentucky . Recently in Illinois
equally summary punishment was inflicted on some gamblers
who had committed an atrocious murder . The rascals were
pursued several miles and when overtaken at least fifty
bullets were lodged in one of them . In the latter case ,
the enormity of the crime and the reckless character of the
perpetrators , with the uncertainty of justice in that
portion of the country are stated as palliation of the
dreadful punishment inflicted .

The Picayune gives a most dreadful account of the
application of the lynch law in the West . It states that a
company of armed men in Phillips County , ( Ark ) set out
in pursuit of a set of counterfeiters and horse thieves ,

whole number , twenty-seven , were tied hand and foot and

officiated or at least took an active part in the execution
of the sentence . This dreadful account is confirmed by the
New Orleans Picayune of yesterday .

hurt from the rough treatment she received while in the
hands of the negroes , the prints of whose fingers were
visible on her neck . We have ever been , and now are ,
opposed to any kind of punishment being administered under
the statutes of Judge Lynch , but when we reflect upon what
must be the feelings of the husband and father , and the
deep anguish which must invade the bosom of the injured
wife and outraged mother , due regard for candor and the
preservation of all that is held most sacred and all that
is most dear to man in the domestic circle of life , impels
us to acknowledge the fact that if the perpetrators of this
excessively revolting crime had been burnt alive , as was
first decreed , their fate would have been too good for
such diabolical and inhuman wretches . - Gallatin Signal ,

Negro , a fiend , truly , who deserved the utmost severity

investigation made of the matter . In Independence ,
recently , it was declared by two of the most influential
citizens of that place , that had they been present when
certain of the participants in the Chavis affair were
arrested , those persons should have been immediately
executed - the writer has reason to believe it was no empty
declaration . As it was , they were maltreated by the mob ;
men too , who have since been proven to have had little to
do with that transaction - while others were coerced to
make statements , under pain of death , which ultimately
may convict the innocent . These are facts which can be
sustained by irrefragable testimony - and it is but justice
when we are considering the causes which lead to the
aspersions of constitutional writers , that we should
consider them and utter against them our condemnation . To
pass them silently over , were to admit the validity of
what is not true - that they are the offspring of a form of
government which is the interest of the anti-republican to
vilify . Though lynch law is so common in this country , it
is by no means peculiar to it , a fact which our
calumniators seem entirely to overlook . In fact , there is
reason to believe that the very term is of European origin

Article:
It is our melancholy duty to record the demise of three of
the most prominent citizens of our country : TIMOTHY DWIGHT
, Mr . ISRAEL Towns , and Mr . NATHANIEL TERRY . Their
deaths were almost simultaneous , occurring within few
hours of each other and will make a lasting impression on
our community . Mr . DWIGHT was a son of the late President
Dwight of Yale College , and has long been one of our most
valuable and respected citizens . Mr . TOWN is the
well-known architect of many of our public structures and
also of the ones in New York , which city has been his
principal residence for several years . Mr . TERRY had been
on a visit to his son in this city , and a few days since
was walking in our streets in his usual health . He has
filled many eminent stations in public life and was highly
respected as a citizen and civilian . MURDER AND Lynch Law
IN MISSISSIPPI . The Natchez Courier of the 18th inst .
contains the particulars of an atrocious murder in
Jefferson County , Miss . with the summary execution of two
of the murderers . It seems that Mr . VIM Boyd , a planter
living near Torry's store , was about to correct one of his
negroes for some offense , when the fellow turned upon his
master and was about to overpower him . The latter called
upon two negro women , who were at work in the same field ,
for assistance but instead of helping they fell upon him
with their hoes and soon put him to death . The three
negroes secreted the body of Mr . Boyd in a brush heap but
in two days time it began to smell , they removed it to a
log pile , set everything on fire , and burnt the body to
ashes . The negroes , suspecting that the absence of their
master would soon be known , assumed boldness enough to
tell some of the neighbors that Mr . Boyd had been absent
for some days that his horse had come up saddled , bridled
, etc . and that they suspected foul play . A search was
immediately instituted by the neighbors , and after some
time they came upon the spot in the field where the combat
had taken place between Mr . Boyd and the negroes . One
thing led to another until the negroes were all arrested ,
when they acknowledged their crime as stated above . The
excitement among the people assembled was very great .
About one hundred persons were collected on Friday last
31st ult . one week from the day of the murder . They
appointed a

Smith , Since our last the official news has been received

citizens of Hancock ! It is unnecessary to say that this

MORE OF THE MORMONS The Mormons are to hold a Convention in
Baltimore on Saturday next . The original intention of the
Convention , as we are given to understand by the public
notice , was to nominate their late leader , Joe Smith , as
a candidate for the Presidency , but his death will render
it necessary to make other arrangements . The ST . Louis
Era of the 1st inst . has the following : The murder of Joe
Smith when in jail , after he had peaceably surrendered
himself , and after the solemn promise of the Governor that
he should be protected and have a fair trial , seems to
meet with very general condemnation . It is another
flagrant instance of the triumph of a mob spirit in the
country . To murder prisoners in cold blood when they are
in custody and when there is nothing to prevent their being
punished to the full extent of their crimes according to
law , is utterly inexcusable . But when mob raised and
excited it knows no bounds , no moderation , but reason and
law are both disregarded . We have often expressed the
belief that mobs and lynch law are improper under all
circumstances , and should not be justified , excused , or
tolerated under any circumstances , and every outbreak
confirms the correctness of this opinion . Joe Smith was
under arrest , with public sentiment strongly setting
against him , there was the most ample opportunity to
convict and punish him legally and severely . And in the St
. Louis Democrat , we find the following : From all the
facts now before us , we regard these homicides as nothing
less than murder in cold blood-murder against the plighted
faith of the chief magistrate of Illinois-murder of a
character so atrocious and so unjustifiable , as to leave
the blacklist stain on all its perpetrators-their aiders ,
abettors , and defenders . Here was Joe Smith in the
custody of the laws to which he had surrendered himself ,
in the confidence that those laws would not be broken to
his injury . He had the promise of preservation and
protection against the very mob violence to which he has
fallen victim . Ought not such faith to have been kept ?
Need we ask , indeed , whether , if there had been special
promise , the law does not always hold out implied safety
to its prisoners from all punishments , save what itself
imposes . Indeed the sight of such solemn pledges , so
shamefully broken , is truly sickening . Why , an Arab , if
you accept his hospitality , and eat of his salt-an
American Indian , if you once pass his threshold in peace ,
will give up his existence before he will suffer his
pledges to be broken . If such acts are to be taken as a
true expression of the spirit which pervades

Judge Lynch . - Four men , RHEA , MITCHELL WHITE , and

of his neck and elsewhere , the marks of which he will

liberality , while few can remember that they have ever

hundred dollars , which he had despatched by him to her .
At Natchez , Capt . Rynders figured extensively and always

rescued the young man from his frightful situation , and
bore him off to safety . Upon the Mississippi Captain
Rynders was well known , and became almost as famous for
his exploits as Paul Jones in the Northern Seas . His
adventurous and hair-breadth escapes would fill a volume .
In female intercourse , and in all matters of love ,
Captain Rynders has always eminently distinguished himself
, and thought , felt , said , done his share . But other
matters of this kind now engross the public attention , we
shall pass them over with brief allusion to an affair of
honor , in which a fair Helen was concerned , which

Wheeling . A gentleman had insulted the wife of one of the
passengers on board . Such circumstance could not transpire
either without the knowledge of Captain Rynders , or

defense of the lady . Challenge to mortal combat was the

his opponent was no little surprised at his prompt
acceptance of his challenge . He chose pistols - - seconds
were selected - - the case became serious to the principals
while outsiders anticipated rich sport and bets ran high
that the Yankee would get shot . At the instance of all the

Article:
OUTRAGE LINCOLN COUNTY . We know that the people of Lincoln
County in this state have been much excited for some months
past over the discovery of the existence of a band of
counterfeiters and horse thieves among them . Several of
them have been at different times arrested , and suspicion
continued to rest against other families . In this state of
tension , a company of Regulators was formed to order the
suspected individuals to leave the county , failing to do
which , summary punishment was inflicted . Two young men of
the family named Turnbull fell under the suspicion of the
Regulators and on Friday last were met at a house in the
county to determine what should be done with them . It is
not known what was agreed upon at that meeting , but from
thence they proceeded to the residence of old Mr . Turnbull
, father of the young men in question . The old man , who
was at his shop , saw them approach and attempted to escape
into the house but was intercepted and caught . Two younger
and the females of the family retreated into the house and
locked the doors . The suspected men were not to be found .
The Lynchers surrounded the house for a time and finally
set fire to it , but it was extinguished by the family .
They then resolved upon more efficient measures and one of
them seized a tool with which , after several blows , broke
the door , and the mob entered . A general fight ensued .
It is not known which party fired first , but two shots
were fired by the young Turner , one of which killed a
young man by the name of Davis . He died only twenty-four
hours after the affray . Another of the mob , named Norrell
or Norris , had been injured in the fracas , having
received a wound in the head with a weapon resembling a
corn knife , which penetrated into the skull and he is not
expected to live long . A third was injured slightly . One
of the Turnbulls was shot in the knee and was dying at the
last accounts . The other was hit in the thigh and was not
expected to survive . One young boy , about sixteen years
old , was missing and unaccounted for . This occurred about
eight miles back of Baileyville between 11 and 12 o'clock
of the day . Great excitement followed throughout the
county . The mob had determined on meeting on Monday to
proceed to the Turnbull's house to exterminate the whole
family , but it is hoped that the efforts being made to
arrest this design will be successful . It is said that the
Regulators are composed of some of the respectable citizens
of the county , but how they can reconcile their
proceedings with a desire to maintain this character is
beyond our comprehension . St . Louis Republican .

Miscreant . - The last Pensacola Gazette gives an account
of the apprehension of a man of the name of Avant , and one
of his confederates named Powers , near Apalachicola . They
were subsequently , taken to Marianna in Jackson County ,
where they were hanged on Friday , the 20th inst , without
the form of trial , and notice was at the same time given
to four gentlemen of the blackleg order , that if they were
found in the place after the lapse of ten hours , they
should share the same fate . If anything can palliate the
atrocity of Lynch law , the circumstances connected with
one of these wretches would almost justify this mode of
punishment . Avant , a monster in human form , and his life
, for series of years , had been marked by crimes of the
deepest dye . He went to Pensacola some years ago , a
fugitive from justice from the State of Alabama , where he
had murdered a Sheriff . He had not been long in Florida
before he attempted the assassination of a citizen , and
being obliged to fly , proceeded to Marianna , where he was
concerned in the murder of another officer . He then fled
to the swamps contiguous to that town , where , up to the
time of his apprehension , and at the head of a band of
outlaws , he perpetrated murder and robbery upon all who
fell in his way . To such length , says the Gazette , had
his audacity been carried , it was considered dangerous to
travel through that part of the State . The peace and
well-being of the community demanded that such villains
should meet with retribution for his outrages , and owing
to the inefficiency of our Territorial government , jails
have been provided for the security of criminals ; he and
his accomplice therefore , in order to ensure their
punishment , were brought to a speedy execution . Those
concerned were actuated by another motive ; there are still
remaining a number of men of the same desperate character
in the swamps about the county and an example which the
slow process of law could not afford was necessary to
strike them with terror . The only sentiment which we can
express on the subject is regret that ?

MURDER IN ARKANSAS-LYNCH LAW . On the night of the 22nd ult
. , a most cowardly murder was perpetrated on the wife of
Shadrack Nichols and their daughter , aged eight years ,
and son four years old , on Hickory Bridge , a short
distance from Helena , Arkansas , while Mr . Nichols was on
a hunting expedition . Suspicion fell on Melson , a yellow
boy , belonging to Mr . Bowman , who is a near neighbor to
Mr . Nichols . The boy was brought to town and lodged in
jail , where he confessed his guilt . We let the Helena
Journal tell the rest of this dreadful tale . The boy
stated that he first went to the house of Mr . Nichols a
week ago last Monday , looked through the window and saw
that Mrs . Nichols had not retired , but was sitting up ,
engaged in sewing . He then returned home , and after
waiting some time , so that she could be asleep , he again
went and murdered her ; breaking her skull with a board ,
and then killed the two children because they awoke . There
were still two children in the house one about eight years
old , daughter of J . Sebastian , Esq , and niece to the
lady murdered , and the other little son of Mr . Nichols .
The fiend then passed his hand over the faces of these two
to see if they were awake . The boy was still asleep , and
, as he supposed , the little girl was also , as she moved
not ; but in this he was mistaken , as the little child had
the courage and presence of mind enough to

had slandered Long and Wade , alleging in his own
justification , that he had been compelled to give names ,
and that the persons named by him were his enemies . Wade
on hearing this , swore that Norris should leave
Russellville immediately Norris ' wife being in delicate
health , and the weather inclement , some of the citizens
remonstrated with Wade , proposing to let Norris remain for
a short time and then send him off quietly . Thereupon Wade
became still more exasperated , and taking Long with him
went to Russellville , hitched his horse in the street ,
and swore he would kill Norris at sight . Some of the
citizens gave Norris money to bear his expenses , and
advised him to depart immediately , which he was about to
do , when he met in the street Long and Wade . They ordered
him to give up his weapons , which he readily did , handing
his pistol to Wade . Long then examined the condition of
his own pistol , and tried the lock deliberately . Norris ,
after giving up his pistol , raised his hands in the most
suppliant manner , walked backwards and begged for his life
on any condition . Wade first drew club , then ran forward
and struck Norris with his fist , which turned him round
facing Long , who , cocking his pistol , took deliberate
aim ,

FORETHOUGHT The most admirable instance of prudential
forethought we ever heard of occurred in Boston a few days
since . Three Irishmen were engaged in taking down a wall
in Mount Vernon Street . The wall fell upon and buried them
. A lady from the opposite side of the street rushed out ,
and calling to those who were rescuing the poor fellows ,
said , ' Bring them in here . Bring them in here . I have
everything ready . I have been expecting this all day . '
The men were carried into her house , and true enough she
had ' everything ready ' bandage , lint , laudanum , and
all . If this is not an instance of ' cool forethought ' we
know not what is . MORE OUTRAGES AGAINST THE MORMONS By the
St . Croix , which arrived last evening from the upper

to assemble and aid in suppressing , as far as possible ,

vicinity of Nauvoo , and those friendly to them . It would
seem that , owing to the scarcity of farming hands in that
neighborhood , few Mormons have been employed by the
farmers in gathering their crops . This has given umbrage
to the lynchers , who recently assembled in considerable
force , and going to a harvest field where some six or
eight persons were at work , some of them new citizens ,
inflicted summary and dreadful punishment on them all . We
further learn from the clerk of the St . Croix , says the
Reporter , that a party of some eighty men from Nauvoo had
turned out , and captured five or six of the principal
rioters . SHOCKING TRAGEDY We learn , says the St . Louis
Organ , that a man by the name of James Duncan , who
resided near Collinsville , ILL . , when a short distance
from his dwelling which he had just left in company with
Miss Page , to go fishing , was attacked on the morning of
the 18th by about forty armed men , some disguised , and
shot by several of them . He was instantly killed , his
body being perforated by thirty or forty bullets . Miss
Page was wounded in the breast . She fought bravely ,
shooting and morally wounding one of the party . She
afterwards fled to the woods , in her fright , and has not
been seen since . The murderers all fled after committing
their diabolical outrage , and no cause for the deed is
known . INDIAN ATTACK It is rumored , says the Iowa Hawk

Article:
LYNCH LAW IN ARKANSAS . The overseer of Col . Walworth ,
rich planter of Chicot County , Ark was murdered about a
year ago , by one of his slaves , who was tried and
convicted of the offense , but subsequently obtained a new
trial . The populace , fearing he would thus escape
punishment , took the law into their own hands , and on the
4th inst . mob repaired to the jail cut down the door ,
notwithstanding the remonstrance and resistance of the
sheriff , entered the prison and forcibly took the prisoner
, led him forth with halter round his neck , and in cold
blood hung him to a tree within 100 yards of the jail .
GAMBLING . Green , the reformed gambler , is lecturing at
Wheeling .

A SERIOUS AFFAIR . - - We are informed that , on Sunday
evening last , in Illinoistown , opposite this city , two
men , coopers by trade , in a drunken frolic , went in a
house where they insulted some ladies . One of the men was
named John Hamer , a German , and the other John Ray , an
Englishman . The owner of the house soon returned , and
finding what had been done , commenced an assault upon them
, and beat them severely with a hoop pole . The two fled to
their boarding house . Subsequently , a number of persons
assembled and went to the tavern where Hamer and Ray were
in bed , took them out , and after severely lynching them ,
put them into a skiff and sent them over to this city . It
is said that the skiff was bored full of holes , and that
they only gave them a couple of sticks to work their way
over on this side . The Irishman , Ray , having made the
most resistance , was worse beaten than Hamer . They
succeeded in reaching this side , and since then , Ray has
died of the injuries received . Yesterday Hamer was taken
to Belleville , to institute proceedings against the
parties engaged in the mob . Ray is represented as a man of
considerable property here , in Peoria , and in Cincinnati
, Ohio . In the latter place , he is said to have a family
. A considerable sum in gold , and a number of deeds and
other papers , left by him , are in the possession of the
keeper of the hotel in Illinoistown , where he was staying
. The subject , we are assured , will undergo medical
investigation . St . Louis Republican

of laws taking the law into their own hands . It should be

regulations which protect their lives and property .

thousand dollars stolen from him . Just when he was on the

, we believe , in a carpet bag , and that deposited for
brief time in a box under his counter of his store and
covered with rubbish it was stolen and a man living in the
same house was suspected . On this suspicion he was decoyed
by his bosom friend James Douglass . A band of enraged
persons seized and assaulted him and took him across the
river . The victim Wiley Brinkley , who was of a
respectable family and a man of bad character , was
tortured by one hundred and fifty lashes on his bare back
to get from him on the old inquisition principle confession
that he stole the money . His family was respectfully
connected and respected among these people and on mere
suspicion publicly tortured to wring confession of guilt
whether guilty or not . Horrifying to think or is
disgraceful to the town whose citizens were engaged in the
affair and most disgraceful to those citizens themselves .
He did not confess . Brinkley kept his bed several days
after his punishment . He finally recovered and as soon as
he could proceeded into the street with loaded double
barreled gun . Mr . Douglass who believed was highly
esteemed shot him . Mr . Douglass was the first of the band

There appears to have lately been some very serious
disturbances of the peace in the neighborhood of Metropolis
City , Illinois . One account says : There is a band of men
back of Metropolis , composed as we learn of the worst
characters , who set at defiance law and order , and are
known by the name of Flatheads . A body of citizens ,
composed of good men , but of course having in their midst
evil characters , are called Regulators , and have been
formed to carry out the law . Some of the Flatheads stole
horses , and one of the Regulators who informed on them ,
was caught , lynched and tied naked to a tree during night
, exposed to the mosquitoes and weather . He died several
days thereafter , but before his death he gave the names of
several persons he had recognized . The Sheriff found them
, but they refused to deliver themselves up unless they
could be tried by Magistrates of their own selections , who
of course would screen them . The Sheriff could not do this
, and they were not arrested at the time . The Flatheads
gathered to the number of about two hundred . The
Regulators assembled , and there was prospect of battle
last week . The Flatheads finally secured and fortified
themselves in a large log house , about twelve miles back
of Metropolis , to the number of about one hundred and
sixty , with two cannons and ammunition . The Regulators
were rallying last Friday ( 27th ult . ) , and had sent to
Paducah and Smithland for cannon , with the determination
to arrest the three men . A grand fight was expected to
come off last Saturday ( 28th ult ) . Another story given
by Dr . Young to the editor of the Cairo Delta , is as
follows : Two or three weeks ago a barn belonging to Mr .
Tolson , was set on fire and burned down . Mr . Tolson and

Execution of All , the murderer of Mr . Anderson , at that
place , on the 20th ult . The murder of Mr . A . was one of
the most atrocious and cold-blooded deeds that we have ever
been called on to record , and it demanded , in the
strongest terms , the severest penalty of the offended law
. But did the enormity of his crime justify a departure
from the due course of law and a resort to Lynch law ? We
think not ; and we are sorry to see such manifestations of
the popular will , in any case , especially where the laws
of our country have provided an adequate and proper
punishment . The presses of other states have always taken
peculiar delight in harping upon the morals of our people ,
and we fear that the recent occurrence at Bentonville will
be caught up as another instance of the laxity of morals in
Arkansas . We sincerely hope this may be the last
manifestation of Lynch law in the state . Bentonville ,
August 21st , 1849 . Dear Clarke : Under the impression
that you are anxious to know the result and finale of Alife
, the murderer of Anderson , I make haste to detail what
took place yesterday before his execution . Early in the
morning , the people began crowding in until it swelled to
a great multitude , such as perhaps never assembled there
before . There seemed to be various opinions as to the
proper course to pursue . Some were for delivering him for
trial and execution , and some for summary punishment . The
near relatives of the deceased were consulted , who said
they delivered him up to the people to do with him as they
pleased . Finally , 11 o'clock came , and there was no
movement towards any definite course—still there was no
excitement amongst the multitude . About this time , the

Article:
LYNCH LAW IN VIRGINIA . - In Culpeper county , on Wednesday
last , a lawless mob assembled at the Court House , and
though resisted by the Sheriff at the Jail door , entered
the Jail and took therefrom by force William Grayson , a
free negro charged with the murder of David W . Miller ,
and hung him by the neck until he was dead . The Superior
Court of Culpeper had twice convicted Grayson and the
General Court had twice granted him a new trial . In the
last opinion the General Court said ' Upon the whole case
of opinion that the testimony not only not sufficient to
prove the guilt of the accused but that is hardly
sufficient to raise suspicion against him . The judgment
must therefore be reversed and a new trial awarded . ' This
infuriated mob , consisting as we regret to learn , in part
of justices of the peace and of members of Christian
churches , have thus by violence reversed the decision of
the highest criminal tribunal in the State and been guilty
of foul cowardly , and endish murder and that too of a
helpless free negro with defend him nor avenge his wrongs .
Grayson , we learn , avowed his innocence on the gallows .
One minute given him in which he was told he was to confess
his guilt this he refused to do and told them to execute
him at once , which they did . This first attempt at Lynch
law in this Commonwealth should be rebuked with firmness
and punished with severity . Unless speedily checked in its
course it must overthrow all government and render life
liberty , and property without protection . It rests with
the legally constituted authorities

of whom applied a pistol to his head while the others
barred the door , and proceeded to rifle his trunk . An
alarm being given , some of the citizens rushed in , and
arrested the whole party . Next day , they were tried by
jury chosen from among the citizens , and sentenced to
receive on the following morning thirty-nine lashes each .
Never having witnessed punishment inflicted by Lynch law
went over to the dry diggings on clear Sunday morning and
on my arrival , found large crowd connected found an oak
tree , to which was lashed man with bared back , while
another was applying raw cowhide to his already gored flesh
. A guard of dozen men , with loaded rifles pointed at the
prisoners , stood ready to fire in case of an attempt being
made to escape . After the whole had been flogged some
fresh charges were preferred against three of the men - two

. These were charged with robbery and attempt at murder on

LYNCHING - - We take the following from the Louisville
Journal of the 18th : The steamer Fleetwood , as has
heretofore been stated , took the surviving passengers and
crew of the ill-fated steamer Kate Fleming to Paducah . One
of the deckhands of the latter was severely wounded by the
explosion , and when he got aboard the Fleetwood laid down
in a berth of one of the deckhands of the Fleetwood . As
soon as the latter came off his watch he ordered the
wounded man out , which being refused , he pulled him out
and beat him , in which , it is said , he was assisted by
the mate . The wounded man was afterwards brought back to
Portland or Shippingsport , where he resided . On the
arrival of the Fleetwood yesterday she was detained
sometime at the foot of the canal . Many of the friends of
the wounded man gathered here , including himself , when he
pointed out the deckhand who had treated him so harshly .
He ( the deckhand ) was taken off the boat by the crowd ,
stripped , tarred and feathered , after which he was
permitted to go . They also looked for the mate , but he
could not be found . We publish communication this morning
, and an extract from the Paducah Journal censuring the
conduct of the captain of the Fleetwood in relation to the
sufferers of the Kate Fleming .

Lynch Law . - Rev . Edward Mathews , traveling agent of the
American Baptist Emancipation Society of New York , while
on a pilgrimage to the residence of Cassius M . Clay of
Kentucky , stopped at Richmond , Kentucky , and made use of
some certain unwholesome expressions relative to the
subject of slavery , which induced the citizens to order
him to leave the town . He left but returned in a day or
two afterwards , whereupon some citizens seized him , and
after ducking him nine times in a horse pond , ordered him
to leave the State . Upon refusing to do so , he was dipped
twice more ; whereupon he promised to leave immediately ,
and took up the line of march on foot for Pennsylvania .

Index Number: preciv369

Article:
We have received files of papers from the Paradise of the
Pacific as its admirers term that auriferous region , and
the picture of its social condition which they convey is
such as to make it verify the description of a purple land
where law secures not life . Recitals of murders and
outrages , promptly succeeded by the wild justice of '
Lynch law , ' as Lord Bacon terms revenge , following each
other in swift succession , crowd the columns of these
papers , and in one only , the Inder , is a voice of
expostulation raised against the summary proceedings of the
mob , who supply the place and exercise the functions of
judges and ministers of justice . That manly and fearless
expostulation against the rule of such Reign of Terror as
the sway of mob law must introduce , we will give tomorrow
. Our space today we reserve for a brief recital of some
few of those popular demonstrations and of the crimes which
provoked them . The stern necessity on unsettled frontiers
of an occasional resort to such examples , to awe the
lawless ruffians who fly like wolves before the advancing
footsteps of law and order , no one can deny , however much
he may deplore the substitution of such irregular and
lawless modes of procedure for the regular administration
of that law whose seal is in the bosom of God and whose
forms afford protection for the innocent against the sudden
gusts of passion . But from the numerous accounts given of
resort to this Lynch law in California of late , as well as
from the following cool commentary which we take from the
Sacramento Transcript , it would seem that it was to be
enthroned as the ' higher law ' of the free State of
California . ANOTHER LYNCHING AFFAIR-The people of

spontaneously arisen en masse to repress crime and punish

little that is new . The 22nd and 23rd of February
witnessed tremendous excitement in San Francisco which for
several hours expected result in the execution , by the
populace , of men named Stuart and Wildyed , noted
scoundrels who nearly murdered Mr . Jansen , a highly
respectable merchant of the city , and robbed him of $ 900
. A meeting of the citizens called at which several of the

selected a jury gave the prisoners a popular trial with the
intention of carrying the verdict rendered and sentence
imposed into effect . The jury disagreed , and the people ,
becoming out with excitement dispersed without attempting
the rescue of the accused from the officers of justice .
The Alta California of February 23rd has the following
reference to this affair : Yesterday was a day of
excitement , such as has been witnessed in San Francisco
since the celebrated Hound trials . The people , who had
previously been aroused of their insecurity from the
frequent murders and robberies occurring in midst followed
, in large masses Stuart and Wildyed who were arrested
charged with being the robbers and attempted murderers of
Mr . Jansen in the Recorder's courtroom , where they were
to be examined . The examination commenced about o'clock .
As it progressed the people began to gather thicker and
occasional bursts of indignation were heard from them .
They were in that state that they could have done almost
anything had not the Washington Guards , who had been
parading during the day , rushed in with fixed bayonets and
mounting the desks and benches drove the people away .
Still there were loud cries of ' Hang them ' , ' Lynch them
' , ' Bring them out ' , etc . The Guards poked their
bayonets at the people , and the people retreated many of
them swearing vengeance on the military . The room was
finally cleared , and the prisoners taken below . When the
Guards left they were hissed and hooted as they marched
into their armory . A great state of excitement prevailed .
The windows of the armory were broken , and much damage
might have been done , when Capt . Bartol came out and said
that the Guards had been called upon to perform their duty
and that they had acted in obedience to that . Monday was
necessary they would march and hang them in the Plaza .
Capt . Bartol was very loudly cheered , and Sheriff Powers
mounting the balcony , addressed the crowd which began to
grow cooler . By the police for riotous conduct but were ,
I believe , released again . There is no mistaking this
general public feeling of indignation . The participants in
this exciting affair are not rowdies , but the majority of
our best citizens . The feeling of insecurity is
entertained by a feeling that the laws afford no protection
and a determination to take matters into their own hands
and make an example . Let nothing be done rashly let
satisfactory proof be given of guilt before punishment is
executed , for the bitterest that the heart could bear in

The quiet of San Francisco was disturbed on the 23rd of
February by a resort to the court of Judge Lynch in the
case of two men who were suspected of having knocked down
Mr . Janson while standing in his own house and robbing him
of $ 2,010 . The jury could not agree on the identity of
the men , and they were therefore left to the regular
courts of law . Two fires occurred on the 2nd and 3rd of
March , both the work of incendiaries . The first was the
burning of the steamers Santa Clara and Hartford . The city
seems to be at the mercy of ruffians and robbers . At the
first fire , there were two men burned to death . At
Sacramento City on the 20th , a party of gamblers were
ungenerously beating a man , because he refused to gamble
with them , when Chas . Q . Myers from Columbus , Ohio ,
interceded for the man and was shot by one of the gamblers
. The citizens assembled at once , tried him , convicted
him of murder , and in five hours after he committed the
deed , he was hanged in the presence of several thousand
persons . The proceedings were conducted by men in
possession of the utmost respect for the urgency .

directions with great rapidity , and continued to rage

minutes Georgetown was as still as that lonely grave upon

Coroner arrived , and upon hearing the story , summoned his
jury for the morning . They met upon the hill and stood
around the unified grave , while the end of a cut cord
dangled above their heads . They exchanged a few words ,
and after laying a slip of paper upon each of the bodies ,
proceeded to fill up the grave . Upon one of the slips was
written ' Murdered by Divine , her husband ' and on the
other ' Died according to the will of God by the justice of
men ' . Yesterday evening , Dr . Lennox , from Scotland ,
was shot while sitting in his office . This morning , the
man who had often menaced his life , was seized on
suspicion , and examined before the proper authorities .
During the examination , several circumstances were
discovered which left scarcely a doubt of his guilt . Just
before the end of this examination , a party of men with
blackened faces , rushed into the room , and their leader
with dexterous whirl of the riata , lassoed the criminal
across the room , and in a moment dragged him outside .
Everyone rushed out . The Sheriff calling all lovers of law
and order to his assistance attempted a recapture . He was
backed by a large party , but many ran to the other side .
After an obstinate fight , the prisoner was retaken and put
under strong guard . No one offered to resist the lynchers
. It is doubtful how the matter will end . The lynchers are
strong in numbers , and may repeat their attempt on a large
scale . The tragedy is yet incomplete , but however the
affair may end , it will still be a tragedy .

Article:
CALIFORNIA INTELLIGENCE . The Alta California of the 15th
April gives the following summary of recent events on the
Pacific side of the Union . Since our last steamer paper ,
the most important event in California has been the repeal
by the Legislature of the odious and disastrous foreign
miners tax bill of last year and proclamation to that
effect by our Governor . The health of this city has been
remarkably good and the same may be asserted of the entire
State for at least any reports have been received . There
can be no healthier country in the world than California .
The annals of crime are about as dark as ever , but the
chances of punishment seem to have improved thereby . The
two men , Worden and Stuart , indicted for the murder and
robbery of Mr . Janson and whose supposed guilt had nearly
been expiated by Lynch law , have been tried before the
district court and found guilty of assault with intent to
kill and robbery . The jury sentenced Stuart to fourteen
years in the penitentiary , the utmost they could give him
. He has since been taken to Yuba County , to be tried for
murder . Worden was sentenced to ten years in the
penitentiary . The Legislature passed a bill authorizing
the Attorney General to enter nolle prosequi in the case of
Charles Robinson and others of Sacramento indicted for
conspiracy and murder in the squatter case , which has been
vetoed by the Governor . There has been a fight in Sonora
growing out of some conflicting miner claims in which one
man , by the name of Davis , was killed and several others
wounded . It was rumored that five men had been killed ,
which proved false . The great fire at Nevada city was not
as destructive to property as was at first reported . Forty
thousand dollars in loss is said will cover the loss ,
which was at first placed at one hundred thousand dollars .
Governor McDougal has been appealed to for his aid in
suppressing the Indian depredations in the Mariposa region
and the Legislature responded to his call upon them by
granting him authority to call five hundred men for that
purpose . Taking a tour for the purpose of acquiring
information upon the subject , he reached the conclusion
that they were not needed and has not called them into
service . One of the Indian Commissioners has recently been
in this city , bringing some of the friendly chiefs with
him , and reports rather favorably of the chances of
forming treaties with the tribes in the vicinity of

tickets , to the State . It is regarded as a good augury
for the future , leading to the anticipation that the
prohibition of gaming will follow in due course of time .
At present the Legislature has not got further than to
provide for licensing gaming , positively prohibiting some
few games , a bill for that purpose being now pending
before them . Many other bills of great importance to the
State and this city are also pending before the Legislature
. Among others , is one relative to land titles in San

Wednesday passed a bill in relation to those subjects ,
which if it should be concurred in by the Senate , would
create great commotion in this city , as it would unsettle
more titles than it would settle . Indian affairs are still
in the same unsatisfactory condition as at the last dates .
Detached parties of Indians visit the camp of the Indian
Commissioners at intervals , but there has been no general
interview nor understanding ; and in the meantime there are
continual reports of incursions of the Indians ,
depredations upon cattle , occasional murders , etc .
Whether the commissioners will succeed in making a treaty
is quite doubtful . Many persons believe the Indians will
procrastinate until the snow melts on the mountains , that
they will then retreat beyond the Sierra Nevada without
making any treaty , and next year will again return to make
their incursions and commit their depredations on the
miners and rancheros . It is believed that they will never
make any treaty which they intend to keep until they are
convinced of the power of the whites by effectual
chastisement . Lynch law , we regret to say , has been
inflicted in several instances , since the last mail . In
one case , two men , who were detected on the Cosumnes
river , stealing horses , were hanged at half an hour's
notice . They gave their names as James Baxter , of Maine
and Charles Simmons , of Mass . The rancheros and miners
possessing property of this kind , have become exceedingly
exasperated . In one locality ( that where these two men
were hanged ) it is said that stock has been stolen to the
value of 100000 , and no mercy will be shown to those who
are detected . Previous to the execution of these two men ,
another man , named Orville Hamilton , had been tried
before Judge Lynch's court for horse stealing , convicted
and sentenced to be hanged the following morning , but
managed to make his escape in the night . A man who was
suspected of aiding him to escape was sentenced to receive
thirty lashes . On the South Fork of the Yuba , man known
as Yankee Jim , whose name was James Knowlton , who had
been detected stealing stock , was also hanged , and
previous to his execution said he could implicate a hundred
others but would not do so . Municipal affairs have been
proceeding much in the same manner usual .

that some Indians during the last winter , while engaged in
a boar hunt , set the world on fire . They ran a bear into
a cave and attempted to smoke him out . They soon observed
the smoke rising from fissures in the mountain for the
distance of half a mile . This was in the mountains above
Fredericksburg . Superstition added terrors to the scene ,
and the Indians came to the settlements to procure
assistance to extinguish the flames . They feared that the
prince of the Internal regions was about to come forth

Article:
New Orleans , June 49 , The steamship Alabama has just
arrived , with two weeks later news from California , the
dates being to May 15 . She came via Acapulco . Her news is
of great importance . A terrible fire occurred in San
Francisco , laying in ashes property to the amount of $
13,100,000 . Among the buildings destroyed , are the Custom
House , Union , Parker's National , New World , City
Demonico's and the Exchange Hotels , also Roses buildings ,
and the offices of the Steamship Co . The fire also spread
to the shipping , consuming large number of vessels which
were lying at their wharves . It was discovered in Clay
street , and ran through about dozen blocks , quickly
spreading to other parts of the city , the greater part of
which lies heap of ruins . Hunter , George , Maine , Centre
, and Eldorado streets are completely gutted . The utmost
conservation prevailed during the fire and thousands were
turned out of house and home , having lost their all .
Measures were about being adopted to relieve the distressed
, and it is feared number of lives have been lost .
Business was not thought of . San Francisco presents
sorrowing and heart-sickening picture . The news from the
mines continues of a cheering character . New discoveries
were being daily made , and the prospects of the mines were
highly favorable . Those working with the quartz crushing
machines were reaping rich reward . In some instances the
average per man was $ 30 to $ 50 per day . Lynch law is
still in force and several new examples have been made .

law in Alston , Florida , with a negro , for murder ,

his life . He had twenty wives living , and had killed

NEW YORK , July 8 , P . M-The steamer Prometheus arrived
here this morning from Nicaragua . She brings California
dates the 30th , and hundred and fifty passengers . The
steamer Tennessee arrived in Panama on the 30th with two
hundred passengers and two and a half million in gold . The
burnt district in San Francisco and Stockton is nearly
built again . The people in respect of business are lively
and the yield of gold unsurpassed . The agricultural
prospects are extremely encouraging . The large fires had
but little effect on the markets . The issue of Indian
difficulties in the northern part of the state continues ,
but commissioners have reported that they met with
excellent success in pacifying the Indians . Both political
parties have made nominations for state officers . The
Whigs have nominated PIERSON B . READING for Governor ,
DRURY P . BALDWIN for Lieutenant Governor , and TOM ROBISON
for Justice of the Supreme Court , E . J . KERNS and B . F
. MORSE for Congress . The Democrats have nominated JOHN
BIGLER for Governor , SAMUEL PURDY for Lieutenant Governor
, and S . HEDERSTEIN for Supreme Court . One case of Lynch
law has occurred . The citizens , hearing that the Governor
intended to commute the sentence of McCav , sentenced to be
hung in Napa , proceeded to the cell and hung him . The
Panama Herald of the 10th ultimo says that an insurrection
lately broke in the provinces of Paso and Tuouerres , but
that insurgents dispersed and escaped to Ecuador after
being defeated with great loss . The Northerner and New
Orleans arrived at Panama on June 3rd - the former with $
6000 in gold and 125 passengers and the latter with $
40,900 in gold and 50 passengers .

Headline: 
Newspaper: Weekly national intelligencer.
Date: 1851-07-26
Article ID: 31_1851-07-26_p3_sn83045784_00415661599_1851072601_0709
LLM Explains: The article describes the lynching execution of Jenkins,

'execution', 'sentence of death', and 'mob lynch law'.
Index Number: preciv353

Article:
The execution of Jenkins - The sudden , unusual and solemn
events which culminated during the night of the instant and
the succeeding morning produced throughout our city
yesterday a feverish state of excitement . The assumption
of authority by a body of respectable citizens and the
enforcement of a sentence of death upon a criminal in the
dead of night without the usual forms of law , may well
create an unusual stir in any community deprived of the
benefit of laws that have caused to be enacted powers to
compel their enforcement and have been at the mercy of
organized gangs of the worst ones that infested city and
preyed upon its inhabitants . Our citizens have been robbed
and murdered , the fair city destroyed by the torch of the
incendiary and hundreds of honest young men stripped of
their hard-earned gains , their prospects in life ruined .
Such a state of affairs could no longer be borne against
every principle of self-preservation and forbearance ;
lenity and mercy had absolutely ceased . The time had
arrived when the people were compelled for the safety of
their lives and the protection of their property to take
the law into their own hands and inflict terrible
punishment on the guilty . Extraordinary circumstances
justified extraordinary measures and the administration of
punishment . The first in the drama has been enacted and
the felon has paid with his life the penalty of his crimes
. We have never been advocates of mob lynch law , snatching
from the hands of legal tribunals the powers legitimately
conferred upon them , of depriving any of the benefit of a
fair and impartial trial by his fellow-men . Yet , in the
present condition of municipal affairs , we cannot
conscientiously condemn the people for the course they have
felt themselves compelled to adopt . Deeply deplore the
occasion for the exercise of such acts , we admit necessity
absolutely demanded the assumption of power . The trial and
conviction of Jenkins was not the act of an inflamed and
excited mob . His case was adjudged with calmness and
determination , his guilt fully established , and the
penalty of death imposed by men respected and esteemed by
their fellows assuming responsibility imposed on them by
necessity , with full perception of their accountability to
their fellow-men and their Maker . Who but He shall adjudge
to condemn them ? We dare not . There may be a difference
of opinion - there undoubtedly is - to the punishment of
burglary and larceny with extreme measures .

New York yesterday from Chagres , via Kingston , ( Jamaica
. ) She brings 81,900 worth of gold on freight , besides

, happened the 22nd of June . It commenced on Pacific
street and went down to Ohio street , burning the City Hall
and City Hospital . All the buildings on Jackson street
were consumed as far as Montgomery . It burnt down
Washington and Dupont streets as far as Sansome , and both
sides of Montgomery street , from Pacific to Washington
street . The new theatre and many houses were destroyed on
Merchant and Clay streets . A large amount of goods which
were removed to the plaza took fire , and were burnt . In
all , five hundred buildings were consumed , many of them
occupied as dwellings by the poorer classes . The estimated
loss is three million dollars . The buildings saved on the
plaza are the Custom-house , the California Exchange ,
Bella Union , Verandah , Eldorado , and Union Hotels .
Messrs . Charles S . Lyons and Bach , of the firm of Bach ,
Burnett & Co . , were burnt to death . The fire was the
work of an incendiary . The burnt district was to be
immediately rebuilt . Some two hundred buildings were
erected during the week preceding the departure of the last
steamer for the Isthmus . Crime still prevailed to a
fearful extent , and there had occurred some additional
cases of administration of Lynch law . Amongst the fresh
outrages reported is the case of Dr . Gunn , editor of the
Sonora Herald , who was dragged from his bed and murdered
by some persons who took offense at an article in his paper
. Two persons connected with his office were also killed ,
and several others wounded . Indian depredations are still
of frequent occurrence . At Rogue River , Capt .
Fitzpatrick and nine men were cut off and all murdered .
The agricultural prospects of the State are favorable . The
reports from the mines are conflicting , but are generally
favorable . The quartz diggings continued to yield well .

Article:
The arrival of the Prometheus brings us fifteen days later
intelligence from California , by way of the newly opened
route of the San Juan de Nicaragua . The prominent , and by
far the most conspicuous feature of the California news is
the prevalence of what would be called on this side of the
continent the supreme authority of Judge Lynch . In San
Francisco it is the execution of the decrees of the
Vigilance Committee of five hundred , not quite so
ceremonious as the old Venetian Council , in the prompt
punishment of criminals , and for the preservation of law
and order . Strange as it may sound in this longitude ,
these off-hand trials and summary executions are , in good
faith , designed for the preservation rather than the
restoration , of law and order . The criminal may be a
murderer , horse thief , burglar , an incendiary , a common
shoplifter , or petty rogue , if the Vigilance Committee
catch him and convict him , he is instantly carried out and
hung up at the nearest convenient tree , or beam , or rope
and tackle . The crime , the pursuit , the apprehension ,
the indictment , the trial , the judgment , and the
execution , may all take place in the same afternoon . The
whole business , in the case of Jenkins , was done in the
course of an evening , by moonlight , and in the case of
Stuart , another Botany convict , tried also as a thief ,
the interval between the commencement of his trial and the
hanging was about five hours . In the case of the Mexican
woman at Downieville who , for fatally stabbing a miner ,
was tried by the popular process established in such cases
, and convicted of murder , the blood of her victim was not
yet cold when the woman , having been tried , convicted ,
and condemned , was swinging lifeless in the air . The
Anglo-Saxon institution of the rope , by sort of ' charge '
or corsican , may

We published , few weeks since , an account of the lynching
of a man named McCoy in Pike County , Ala , on the ground
that he was an abolitionist . By the following article from
the Tuskegee ( Ala ) Republican , it will be seen that the
same man has again suffered violence at the hands of a
pro-slavery mob . An abolitionist in Hockley on Wednesday .
Last , McCoy , the abolitionist who had been treated so
like a stranger by the good people of Pike , made his
appearance in our town . This news soon spread , and about
a dozen of our citizens constituted themselves a special
committee to wait upon him and inquire into his whereabouts
and business . He denied being an abolitionist or that he
was the same man that was ducked in Pike , or that his name
was McCoy . The proof , however , was conclusive , and
thereupon a jury of good and lawful men was empaneled to
inquire into his case and if the facts were found against
him , to assess the punishment . The jury having retired ,
and evidence being adduced , there was no doubt left that
he was the individual that he was suspected of being . It
was likewise proved that he had talked abolition the night
before , besides misbehaving in other ways , and that he
had talked abolition that very morning in town . The
verdict , therefore , was that he be taken to the town pump
and after being well pumped upon , that he be rode on a
rail out of the corporate limits of the town . The whole
sentence was carried into effect in a most becoming manner
, the boys accompanying the procession with music on the

Two Men Lynched - A letter from Sonora , dated June 10th ,
gives the following information : This has been truly an
eventful day . While quietly in the office attending to
business , our ears were suddenly startled by the quick
report of the unmistakable Colt's six-shooter . The street
was soon cleared , and on taking a look saw the Marshal ,
Mr . McFarland , backing down the street , exchanging shots
with a Chileno whom he had attempted to arrest . The
Chileno was still advancing when one of the constables
fired and shot the assailant in the breast , killing him
dead . During this melee , the Mexicans and Chilenos
attempted rescue , whereupon the fight became general
between them and the Americans , which resulted in the
killing of two Mexicans , and wounding three others . The
fight soon after subsided by the foreigners retreating . It
is expected that there will be a general attack on the
Americans tonight , but if they should wake up the Yankees
they will ' catch a Tartar ' as we are all prepared . Truly
these are exciting times . Two of the murderers of Capt .
Snow were arrested in town this afternoon , and on being
tried and calmly convicted , they were taken off and hung
by the people . They were subsequently buried in the same
grave they had dug for their victim . The people in their
might are rising , and the account they will have to settle
with the murderers and thieves who infest us will be a
warning to them in the future .

FROM CALIFORNIA . - By the arrival of the Empire City , the
Picayune has received full files of San Francisco papers to

Francisco ; that is to say , the Vigilance Committee has
surrendered its power to the authorities . The last
criminal arrested by them was a man named Adams , whose
guilt was definitely ascertained by the committee , though
he was afterwards surrendered to the authorities . Lynch

and while he was putting it up Graham shot him with a shot
gun , five buckshot lodging in the back part of the head
and neck . Graham then robbed him of seven hundred dollars
in gold dust , and started for Sacramento . In about half
an hour Leslie recovered sufficiently to crawl to a cabin
about a mile distant and gave the alarm , when a party
immediately set out in pursuit of Graham , and succeeded in
capturing him about three miles below this place , and
recovering the gold . He was taken back to Greenwood Valley
, when a court of the people was called , a jury of twelve
men chosen , and the trial commenced . Everything was
conducted in a cool and deliberate manner . After a patient
hearing , which lasted from 10 o'clock at night till 11 the
next day , the jury retired and soon returned with a
verdict of guilty , and sentenced the prisoner to be hung .

o'clock this evening was taken to a tree in the rear of the

Article:
LYNCHING . - A case of lynching came off at Sweetland's the
past week under the following circumstances . A company of
Kanakas were stopping in that vicinity , among whom was a
boy of some twelve years of age . One of the Kanakas , for
some slight offense , severely beat the boy , and finally
kicked him in the breast with such violence as to deprive
him of consciousness . The miners took the Kanaka and
sentenced him to be hung , but afterwards concluded to let
him off with fifty lashes . These were soundly administered
and he was sent on his travels . The boy was taken up for
dead , but by the skill of a physician resident there , was
recovered , and now exhibits no other marks of his rough
treatment than a habit of stuttering .

Index Number: preciv337

Article:
other , rapidly increasing list of trials condemnations and
executions , perpetrated with relentless severity by the
summary action of the people . To those who are
unacquainted with the difficulties under which California
has labored , ever since the adoption of her State
Constitution , the latter alternative may appear even more
terrible than the former ; and a course dictated in fact by
the most awful necessity which can be imposed upon any
community , may seem little else than the lawless outbreak
of unbridled popular passion . We have been somewhat
sharply taken to task by some of our contemporaries for
justifying the motives of the San Francisco Committee of
Vigilance , and the members of the Committee themselves
have been made the subject of violent denunciation ; yet
every successive arrival from California proves more
clearly the justice of what we first asserted that the
Lynch Law now in operation is not Mob Law , but the result
of a universal sentiment of order , conscientious belief
that it cannot be obtained by trusting to the regular
authorities , and sense of danger which impelled them to
immediate action . We have professed our inability to judge
, at this distance , whether other means might not have
been employed to enforce the laws avoiding courses which
must be always hazardous to the future peace of society ,
even when the sternest exigency compels it . The
disclosures which we publish today , show clearly the
reality of the dangers to which the Californians were
exposed ; they show how nearly hopeless was the reliance to
be placed on ordinary operations of law . So far as the
evidence goes , they prove , at least , that there have
been sufficient reasons for the action of the Committee of
Vigilance , to exonerate them from the violent charges
which have been made against them on this side of the
Continent .

THE LYNCH LAW EXECUTERS IN CALIFORNIA . It will be
remembered that when first the intelligence reached us that
so-called vigilante committee had been formed at San
Francisco on Lynch-law principles , and that , instead of
endeavoring to make the courts purer and more efficient ,
the committee assumed to be independent of and superior to
the law and its officers , we deprecated the whole movement
as containing the very essence of anarchy and misrule , as
in fact substituting violence and an irresponsible
authority for recognized institutions with officers
accountable to the people . We saw in that step the
introduction of a principle that sooner or later must work
mischief , for principles are indestructible and operative
, and must produce results . The emergency of the case was
pressed deeply and told that in no other way could life and
property be protected , that the committee were all
honorable , that the popular sentiment was entirely with
them , a fact which undoubtedly at one time was true ,
shown by the hideous longing for an exception evinced by
the populace when Whitaker and McKenzie hung . The ground
assumed in these columns with reference to the vigilance
committee system was , that it was wrong in principle and
would therefore eventually become evil in practice , that
assumption of authority vested elsewhere by the people ,
and by them legitimately transferred , to the vigilance
committee , that it was not merely a location of
magisterial and executive power but violent opposition to
the laws and constitution of the State , despotic
administration of life and death , and of unheard-of
punishments , and of chamber examinations and convictions
all tending to demoralize and debase the people , by making
them familiar with summary convictions

Article:
We learn from an extra from the S . Herald , furnished us
by Adams & Co that a most horrible tragedy was perpetrated
at Campo Seco , or Turnersville , last night about 11
o'clock . A party of Mexicans , four in number , in company
with two Englishmen and one American , entered a tent where
there lived four Americans for the purpose of robbing them
. Although the latter were unarmed , they made desperate
resistance with stools and benches . One of the Americans
in the tent was killed , and all the rest more or less
wounded , one of them so dangerously he is not expected to
live . A Mexican was caught today , and on confessing
having been accessory to the facts was hung by order of
Judge Lynch .

A GREAT VILLAIN . - The man John B . Harden , lately hung
by lynch law in Milton , Florida , with a negro , for

death , for he had been a villain all his life . He had

he was sorry for was that he was not permitted to live long
enough to kill four more . Repentance and remorse were
strangers to him . He said his father and brother had both
been hung .

which was granted . While sleeping under the corridor ,

at one blow , killing him instantly . The assassin then
attacked the Peruvian with the same weapon and cut and
bruised him in such manner that no hopes are entertained of
his recovery . On learning what had taken place , the
citizens sallied out in pursuit of the Indian in the
morning , and soon found him ; but it was only after a
desperate resistance that he was finally captured . He was
firmly bound , and as it was determined to execute him
immediately , he was told that he had but an hour to live .
He said his sentence was perfectly right , that he deserved
death , etc . , but asked that his hands might be loosed ,
that he should not be kept suffering in the meantime . This
request was granted , but no sooner were his hands at
liberty than he used them to hurl a large stone at one of
his captors , which struck him with such force as to
inflict a severe wound . The citizens were now so

Article:
A GAMBLER LYNCHED ON THE Mississippi . - The Louisville
Courier states that recently a party of returned
Californians left New Orleans for St . Louis and that
during the trip a gambler named Williamson induced one of
them to enter his state-room , and persuaded him to take a
glass of liquor , which it appears was drugged with
morphine . The Californian was immediately taken ill , but
soon recovered , and would have shot Williamson dead on the
spot but for the interference of the captain . On reaching
a place named Hickman , the passengers took the law into
their own hands , and seizing the offender , proceeded to
the woods , tied him up to a tree , and gave him
sixty-seven lashes on his bare back , and turned him loose
. It is said that every blow brought blood from the poor
wretch , whose screams could be heard a mile .

terrible earnest . The report was that a digger had killed

military and police , the mob seized the aggressor and hung
him at once , over the hole where he had committed the
murder . The following is taken from the letter of a
