other similar deaths unrecorded . It is said that Conway
was murdered on account of his participation in the
lynching of Steve a short time since , Pancho being the
partner and bosom friend of the latter .

of the State . Since then , the press and telegraph has
given assurance for nearly a week that the attempt of the

or the apologist of Lynch law . We should rather see men
render their influence and service to sustain the laws , by
devoting an occasional day in the capacity of jurors , than
forming vigilance committees , and taking even the blackest
criminals from the hands of the law . We are not yet
prepared to make so dangerous and improper use of the press
, as to recommend the application of the fearful code .
There is no assassination so malignant , no crime so bold ,
but the law properly and fearlessly administered , will
reach it . The proper administration depends upon the
people . Who amongst us is prepared to set aside the
guarantees of the constitution under which we live ? Are we
prepared to admit by taking the law into our own hands ,
that our system of government has failed , and that the
ballot-box is no longer the bulwark of our freedom ? It is
true that in the present case the assumption by the people
of the most solemn responsibilities , although it cannot be
justified , is extenuated by series of wrongs , expressions
and spoliations , unparalleled in the history of society in
the U . S . Outrages the most gross have been unblushingly
perpetrated in open daylight , and in the face of the
constituted authorities . San Francisco has been the scene
of a thousand homicides , and of but two convictions by due
process of law . Professional gamblers and hardened
villains have shot down respectable men with impunity , and
the courts have failed to convict , either through their
own corruption or inefficiency . The talents of
ex-Congressmen , lured by the money of prostitutes , has
been exerted in defense of assassins , gamblers and thieves

Headline: 
Newspaper: Butte record.
Date: 1856-06-28

LLM Explains: The article mentions 'Lynch law' and the hanging of Charles

Index Number: preciv199

Article:
high-handed measures , outrages and crimes . They use their
momentary power to gloat over , insult and trample upon the
people of the State . It is difficult to understand what
adequate motives there can be alleged for all this violence
, commotion and crime . The two most prominent reasons
given by the Vigilance Committee are the uncertainty of the
punishment of crime by the illegal tribunals , and the
perpetration of election frauds by the officers of
elections . If crime is not punished in San Francisco , it
is not for the want of able and upright judges . It is
owing to the corruption of the juries . It will be admitted
that the jury system in San Francisco has not always been
remarkable for purity . But whose fault is this ? That of
the very merchants and shopkeepers who now mainly
constitute the Vigilance Committee . They decline or shirk
the duty of serving on juries on any frivolous pretext . It
is well known that a distinguished Judge in that city not
long since adjourned his court for the day , alleging that
he was sickened with hearing business men commit perjury in
swearing to excuses to evade serving on juries . This is an
evil which the members of the Vigilance Committee might
easily have corrected by the simple discharge of their duty
as citizens , and the reform would have produced a pure and
efficient administration of justice . The evil is not
likely to be made less by superseding as judges , such men
as Judges Morton , Hager , and Shattuck , by the creatures
of the Vigilance Committees . If business men would
discharge their legal obligations as good citizens , there
could be no necessity for Lynch law and revolution . But
the greatest monstrosity of all is that some of the very
men who , as jurors , refused to concur in a verdict for
the conviction of Charles Cora , subsequently as members of
the Vigilance voted to hang him . As to election frauds ,
bribery and ballot box stuffing , there is no doubt that it
has been a monstrous evil in San Francisco . These crimes
have been resorted to a frightful extent by all political
parties ; but the punishment has fallen exclusively upon
the offenders in one . Some of the most notorious
perpetrators of election frauds are prominent members of ,
and sympathizers with , the Vigilance Committee . They are
honored and protected members of that treasonable
association . Since they have created a new punishment
unknown to the law , that of banishment , why do they not
mete out justice to the offenders of all parties ? Again ,
the Legislature at its last session had passed an efficient
remedy as to San Francisco , by naming several gentlemen of
character , selected from all parties , to appoint the
Judges and Inspectors of elections . Is mere revenge in
furnishing past frauds on the ballot box a sufficient
motive for all this rebellion and civil commotion ?

WAsHINcroN, July 20, 1856.
Newspaper: Washington sentinel.
Date: 1856-08-02
Article ID: 10_1856-08-02_p2_sn82014835_00415661514_1856080201_0862
LLM Explains: The article describes an incident where there was
significant public excitement about the recapture of slaves
and the difficulty of preventing their captors from being
lynched by enraged citizens. It specifically mentions the
threat of lynching, and although it did not happen, the
potential for such an event categorizes this article under
past lynching events, making it best fit category 1.
Index Number: preciv196

Article:
On the 10th , 11th , and 18th of April , 1848 , great
excitement prevailed in this District , arising from the
fact that a large number of slaves had been stolen from the
neighborhood by certain white men in command of a pirate
schooner , which had brought wood to this city . On Sunday
, April 16 , the steamboat Salem , Captain Baker , and
about thirty citizens started in pursuit , and discovered
the schooner Pearl , lying in Cornfield Harbor , at the
mouth of the Potomac , on Monday morning . The slaves ,
seventy-seven in number , were asleep below , and Edward
Sayres , the Captain , Chester English , and Daniel Drayton
, ( white men ) were also below . The Salem rang alongside
, boarded the Pearl , fastened down the matches , and
secured the fugitives and kidnappers , before they could
resist and returned with them to Washington on Tuesday ,
the 18th of April . The fugitives consisted of thirty-eight
men , twenty-six women , and thirteen children .
Seventy-four were from the District , and three from
Virginia . The excitement was so great upon the arrival of
the parties at the steamboat wharf , that it was difficult
to prevent the enraged citizens from seizing the culprits
and lynching them on the spot . In the Union of April 18 ,
1848 , you will find a full statement of the above fact ,
with the names of the owners of most of the slaves . The
following record shows the action of the Criminal Court in
the case : Criminal Court of the District of Columbia , for
the county of Washington . March Term , 1849 . United
States vs . Daniel Drayton . May 9th . Convicted of
transporting slaves in 73 cases , and sentenced by the
Court in each case to pay a fine of $ 140 and costs , one
half of the fine to the owner of the slave , according to
the Act of Assembly of Maryland , of 1796 , ch . 67 .
Ordered to be committed to the Jail of Washington county ,
till fines and costs are paid . Same number of cases vs .
Edward Sayers and fined 500 and cost in each and committed
as above . Test : JOHN A . SMITH , Clerk . Great efforts
were made by the Abolitionists to procure the release of
the prisoners through the clemency of the President , Mr .
Fillmore , but without success , until after General Scott
was nominated . Then , when Mr . Fillmore had nothing more
to expect from the South , the Know-Nothing party not
having been originated , you will find the following entry
relative to Drayton and Sayers , viz . 18th August , 1852 .
Discharged from Jail by the President of the United States
Millard Fillmore . Test : JOHN A . SMITH , Clerk . It
becomes of importance next to know who was instrumental in
procuring the release of these wholesale robbers and

LLM Explains: The article describes the impact of the Vigilance Committee
and mentions specific lynching events that have happened,
particularly referring to the executions in San Francisco,
and the atmosphere surrounding it. Key terms include 'lynch
law execution' and 'execution of the first Vigilance
victims'.
Index Number: preciv197

Article:
Existence has created and is now fostering in the community
. No subject that has ever agitated the public mind has
been fraught with so much evil , or given rise to such
acrimonious feelings , or excited the public mind to such a
pitch . Neighbors regard each other with perfect
astonishment , and distrust and foul suspicion is
engendered among those whose intercourse has formerly
evidenced the most honorable feelings and friendly
relations . The press , too , has felt the influence of the
mob at the Bay , and forget that their appeals should be to
the judgment of the people , instead of their passions ,
and indulge in the most bitter and acrimonious feelings .
It is almost impossible to open paper from below , without
reading the disgusting details of lynch law execution , or
the speculations of mob editors upon the next victim of the
tyrants who now rule San Francisco with the secret conclave
and the bayonet , to the everlasting disgrace of our
republican institutions . As if to add fresh fuel to the
feelings already agitating the State , the Atlantic papers
are now favoring the California public with the
republication of their proceedings , and Casey and Cora
have just been rehung by the arrival of the last Steamer .
Editors , who have remained tranquil during the widest
political excitements through which the country has passed
, have yielded to the deleterious influence arising in San
Francisco , and given vent to their pent-up feelings , in
bitterness of language in strong contrast with their former
course . The atmosphere is impregnated with lawlessness ,
the times are demoralized , and the public sense vitiated
and perverted into unhealthy channels . In proof of this ,
is the chronicled fact that hundreds rushed to San
Francisco to witness the execution of the first Vigilance
victims . On the circulation of the rumor that some
unfortunate individual is to be hanged at a certain hour by
the Vigilance Committee , and men , women and children ,
old and young , hurry to the Vigilance Bastille for the
purpose of witnessing the expected execution . The depraved
taste which seeks for its qualification the dying agonies ,
the throes and convulsions of a fellow being hurried into
eternity , is a disgrace to humanity . When we find women
bringing their young children to feast their eyes on the
horrid spectacle , ladies in their carriages with their
lorgnettes ready to be used to bring the object nearer ,
morals , refinement and sensibility , in

MRS . STOWE'S NEW BOOK . Another chapter from the advanced
sheets Of Mrs . Stowe's new anti-slavery work to be issued

LYNCH LAW . The rays of the afternoon were shining through
the fringe needles of the pines . The sound of the
woodpecker reverberated through the stiffness of the forest
, answering to thousand wood hand notes . Suddenly along
the distant path , a voice was heard singing and the sound
comes strangely through the dreamy stiffness : ' Jesus
Christ has lived and died , what's all the world beside !
This to know is all I need to know life indeed . Teach me
this , and this alone ; Christ for me has lived and died ,
Christ forever dignified . ' And , as the last lines fell
upon the ear , a figure riding slowly on horseback , comes
around the bend of the forest path . It is Father Dickson
in the habit of this good man , much of whose life was
spent in solitary journeys , to use the forest arches for
that purpose for when they seemed so well designed with the
reins loose over the horse's neck , and pocket-Bible in his
hand . Occasionally he broke out into snatches of song like
the one which we heard him singing a few moments ago . As
he rides along now , he seems absorbed in mental prayer .
Father Dickson , in truth , had cause to pray . The
plainness of speech which he felt bound to use had drawn
down upon him opposition and opprobrium , and alienated
some of his best friends . The support which many had been
willing to contribute to his poverty was entirely withdrawn
. His wife , in feeble health , was toiling daily beyond
her strength ; and hunger had looked in at the door , but
each day prayer had

LLM Explains: The article offers detailed descriptions, using terms like

Index Number: preciv193

Article:
We stated the case the other day of Mr . Strickland & Co .
, the booksellers of Mobile , who were driven by the means
process of Judge Lynch's Court from that city , wisely
refusing to remain and stand trial , and await judgment for
the crime of being suspected of selling to the inhabitants
of that city books of anti-slavery tendency . It appears
that a part at least of their offense consisted in
furnishing their customers with books by Mr . Frederick
Douglass , but whether it was confined to this is not
stated . The works of Washington , of Jefferson , of
Franklin , the Declaration of Independence , and the
history of the formation of the very Constitution which the
shareholders claim having been an invention , like the
cotton-gin , mainly for their behoof and benefit , may as
well be included in the process as the writings of Mr .
Garrison or of Judge Jay . It is altogether likely that
Messrs . Strickland & Company are Northern men , and it is
more than probable that they were owners of no slaves .
Still , it would have made no difference had they been born
Alabamians , and had been patriarchs on their own account .
The fact of owning slaves and being Southerner by birth
forms no safe-conduct for a man who is so much as suspected
of having ever looked at the North Star , and so been
misled into paths that lead toward forbidden ground . Mr .
Sherwood of Galveston , in Texas , is a proof in point of
this truth . That this gentleman is a stakeholder of
prominence , and otherwise a citizen of distinction , is
proved by the fact that he was elected by that loyal town
to represent it in the Lower House of the Texas Legislature
. He was not only a slave-holder but a Southern man by
birth

The LYNCHING AT ANGELS . - As various opinions , concerning
the Lynching at Angels , are entertained , on account of
the different statements of the affray , which have been
circulated , we took some pains to publish the official
evidence , taken before the Coroner's Jury . We are also
indebted to our special correspondent for the account which
he has furnished ; he being present during the whole time
of the excitement , his statement may be relied on .

Newspaper: San Andreas independent.

Article ID: 3_1856-10-18_p2_sn85025090_00279557463_1856101801_0018
LLM Explains: The article discusses the judicial outcomes of recent
criminal cases and mentions lynching in the context of
justice being served by law to avoid lynching by a mob.
This suggests a historical lynching context related to

Index Number: preciv191

Article:
DISTRICT COURT - The District Court recently in session at
Mokelumne Hill adjourned on Thursday last . Some of the
most important criminal cases before the Court were
promptly disposed of . In Judge Creanor , the judicial
ermine is honored by the man , and if every Judicial
Officer in this State had the moral firmness and
unflinching integrity of Chas M . Creanor , criminals would
not , as is so often exemplified , rush from the just
vengeance of an outraged people into the protecting arms of
the Law , to get clear of being Lynched . People vs . Pedro
Espinosa Murder : The Jury returned a verdict of guilty of
Manslaughter . The Judge sentenced the prisoner to eight
years confinement in the Penitentiary . People vs . Wall
Stewart Murder : Verdict by the Jury , Guilty in the First
Degree , and the prisoner was sentenced to be hung on the
9th of December . People vs . Santiago Diaz Murder : This
case was of a new trial ordered by the Supreme Court . The
case was postponed until the next Regular Term .

THE RAT-CATCHER II While the greater portion Of population
are at sea John Chinaman like the scavengers or rag-pickers
A other distinct class Of persons have in our midst wide
awake and pursuing his calling with zeal worthy
commendation In any Of the higher walks Of life He what

Scotch terrier in the approved method of discovering and Of
taking them The wharves along the waterfront Of the city
nightly frequented by them but in they seek the isolated in
the outskirts Of the city where the rats tten the garbage
thrown from the houses set their traps These traps square
boIes about feet long and Of the same width , and eighteen
inches deep the top and bottom constructed Of wood and the
sides ingeniously interwoven with wire like net Each has
several openings the sides with the wire inverted that when
a rat enters , it is almost impossible for him make his
escape by the way he came in Each Chinaman with any kind Of
good luck very often succeeds in taking from two to three
hundred rats a night These are sold to the parties who
employ them at so much per dozen who derive considerable
profit from the business The skins dried and cured and
afterward tied up in small packages Of fifty each ready for
exportation . They to Paris and there manufactured into
what called kid gloves and returned to US as Alexandres

Article:
stripped himself to the work of massacre ! Oh then , but it
was vision both glorious and dreadful to behold the orator
. His actions , before graceful as the wave of golden
willows in the breeze , grew impetuous as the motion of an
oak in the hurricane . His voice became trumpet filled with
wild whirlwinds , deafening the ear with crashes of power ,
and yet intermingled all the while with the sweet under
song of the softest cadence . His face was red as drunkards
, his countenance looked haggard like that of maniac ; and
ever and anon he flung his bony arm on high , as if
grasping after thunderbolts . He drew picture of murder in
such appalling colors , that in comparison , hell itself
might be considered beautiful . He painted the slanderer so
black that the sun seemed dark at MONDAY when shining on
such an accursed monster , and then he fixed both portraits
on the shining brow of Hopkins and then he nailed them
there forever . The agitation of the audience nearly
amounted to madness . All at once the speaker descends from
his perilous height . His voice wailed out for the murdered
, and described the sorrows of the widowed living , the
beautiful Mary , more beautiful every moment , as her tears
flowed faster , till men wept , and lovely women sobbed
like children . He closed by strange exhortation to the
jury , and through them to the by ISLANDERS . He entreated
the panel , after they should bring in their verdict for
the plaintiff , not to offer violence to the defendant ,
however richly he might deserve it , in other words ' not
to lynch the william Hopkins , but leave his punishment to
God ' . This was the most artful trick of all , and the
best calculated to ensure vengeance . The jury rendered
verdict for fifty thousand dollars , and the night
afterwards Hopkins was taken out of bed by lynchers , and
beaten almost to death . As the court adjourned the
stranger made known his name , and called attention to the
announcement ' John Taylor will preach at this place
tonight , at early candle light ' . The crowd of course all
turned out , and Taylor's sermon equalled , if it did not
surpass the splendor of his forensic efforts . This is no
exaggeration . I have listened to Clay , Webster , and
Calhoun , to Dewey Tinge , and Bascom , and have never
heard anything in the form of sublime word even remotely
approximating the eloquence of John Taylor , massive as
mountains and wildly rushing as cataract of fire . And this
is the opinion of all who have ever heard the marvellous
man .

MORE LYNCHING IN MONTEREY The Santa Cruz Sentinel of
Saturday last says : We have been kindly permitted to
extract the following from private letter to a citizen of
this place , written under date of the 3rd inst . On
yesterday we heard of camp of Spaniards , in the willows
little below the Tom Blanco , crossing , on the South side
of the river ; so we made up company of men , and went to
arrest them . They resisted several shots were fired on
both sides . There were two Spaniards was shot dead , and
the other gave himself up . The one killed is said to have
been concerned in the murder of Wall and Will Hanson . They
were notorious rascals . The one killed had shot man in
Monterey , and they both intended to leave for parts
unknown , as soon as they could learn what Anastacio Garcia
, who was brought from below last week , had to say . These
fellows had stolen saddle from us few days before . The one
taken had stolen Smith's mule last spring . So we took them
out to the side of the road , laid one down , and hung one
up ; left the dead to take care of themselves , and have
heard nothing from them since .

Headline: LYNCH LAW IN KANsAs.-Tbe St Iopi. Republican, of the 14th ins, sa,
Newspaper: The true Democrat.
Date: 1856-12-02
Article ID: 24_1856-12-02_p2_sn84022882_00414212438_1856120201_0036
LLM Explains: The article describes an event where two men were hung by
'Lynch law' on Pottawatomie Creek in Kansas for crimes of
robbery, making it a specific lynching event that has

Index Number: preciv185

Article:
Republican of the 14th instant , says : A gentleman from
the southern part of Kansas informs us that one of those
acts not unusual in all the new territories of the west
recently occurred on Pottawatomie Creek , in Kansas
territory . Various robberies had been out and apprehended
two persons who it was believed to be concerned in robbing
an able citizen named Briscoe Davis of all his property ,
and the widow Conet of $ 100 in cash , horse , and all her
property . After due examination they were hung with the
ropes taken from the necks of the animals stolen by them .
One of the men thus hanged by virtue of Lynch law , is
supposed to have borne the name of Partridge , and has been
a quite conspicuous character in that territory . The name
of the other was not known . These men , it is said ,
executed for crimes committed by them , and not from any
political consideration .

of the death-warrant , by the Sheriff , the prisoner
remained calm and motionless but became very pale and
considerably agitated as the rope was adjusted about his
neck . As he neared the dark valley of the shadow of death
he became somewhat confused , and called rather
incoherently , " mercy my father in heaven my children , "
etc . He asked anxiously , " won't they have prayer " After
which the cap was drawn over his face , and brief but
affecting petition was addressed to the Throne of Grace .
As the minister pronounced the word " Amen , " the fatal
drop fell , and the spirit of Nathan Cottle took its flight
to another sphere of existence . The prisoner was dressed
in suit of black . A white muslin cap was used . The body ,
after hanging until pronounced dead by the attending
physician , was taken down and buried short distance from
the scaffold . Much credit is due the Sheriff and his
assistants , for the admirable manner in which all
preparations had been made . The very best of order
prevailed throughout . Opinions varied considerably as to
the number present . Some thought there were fifteen
hundred or two thousand ; but probably about eight hundred
would be the best estimate . Of this number , some eighteen
or twenty we are sorry to say were females . This is the
first execution by the officers of the law , that has ever
taken place in Annador county ; but large number have been
executed under sentence from Judge Lynch . The reporter of
this is under particular obligations to Sheriff Durham and
Deputy Sheriff Houghtaling , for their kindness in
furnishing every facility for taking notes .

Index Number: preciv181

Article:
LYNCH LAW IN VIRGINIA-THE SLAVE EXCITEMENT . - During the
late Christmas holidays , information having been received
in Scottsville that Benjamin Hunt , a citizen of this
county , residing near that village , had been using
language tending to encourage insurrectionary movements
among the slave population , a deputation of several
citizens repaired to his residence to arrest him . On being
brought to the village , the charges against him were
subjected to a rigid investigation , and during the
examination , the following facts were incontrovertibly
proved . One witness testified that he had heard Hunt say
that the white man had no more right of ownership in the
negro than the negro had in the white man , and if it came
to the test , he would sooner fight for the negroes than
the white people . Another witness testified that he had
heard him say that he would have rejoiced in the election
of Fremont , as in that event the negroes would have been
freed ; and that in case of a collision between the whites
and blacks , he would take the side opposed to the white
people ; whereupon the indignant populace seized and
compelled him to be ridden upon a rail , borne by several
stout negro men , through the streets . When they had
paraded him through the streets to their hearts ' content ,
they were ordered to take him to a more retired part of the
village and administer an application of tar and feathers ,
which they did with equal satisfaction and delight . When
this was done he was ordered to leave the county within ten
days , and seek his fortunes where the institutions were
better adapted to his anti-slavery feelings . -

mark . Out of 900,000,000 pounds of cotton imported into

. Narrow Escape of the Mexican President - President

Lynch Law in Iowa - On the 29th ult . , an armed body of
from fifty to sixty men , passed through Maquoketa , Iowa ,
to Dewitt , where they took from the jail two men , named
Barger and Carroll , who were awaiting trial for murder .
They took their prisoners to the town of Andrew , where
they hung Barger , who was alleged to have brutally
murdered his wife , but returned Carroll to the custody of
the officers of the law . No arrests have been made .
Kansas a Free State - A letter received yesterday from a
source in Kansas entitled to the most implicit confidence
states that three-fourths at least of the present settlers
are in favor of making Kansas a free state , including
nearly one half of the emigrants from slaveholding states .

LYNCH LAW IN ADAMS COUNTY The Natchez Courier , of last
Saturday , has the following account of a lynching
operation in that vicinity : According to general report ,
there was kind of special Assizes Court held night before
last below the city , near the site of old Fort Rosalie ,
and beneath the early light of the waning moon . It was one
of those courts over which neither our high court or
circuit judges preside , but which are called into being
occasionally by the necessities of the case . It seems that
quite a number of valuable negroes have been stolen or run
off from this neighborhood within a few weeks back . The
absenting slaves are generally believed to have been
enticed away by white men , under the pretense of a safe
conduct to a northern clime , but who , after getting
possession of the negroes , have heartlessly sold them .
One or two slaves thus decoyed away , have been recovered ,
while others are still absent . It is generally reported
that a man by the name of Foster , or who gave himself that
name , and who had been implicated in this wholesale
business of negro stealing , was taken somewhere below Fort
Rosalie , the night before last , and had administered to
him most royal cow-hide flagellation , well laid on , after
which a skiff was rendered to him with free permission to
travel to some other vicinity where negro stealing was
differently punished . Under the effect of his punishment ,
it is understood that he made full confession , implicating
some other persons in this vicinity . We presume the latter
will find Natchez an undesirable place of residence .

Article:
At Florence , Nebraska , seven men were lately condemned to
death by Judge Lynch for the crime of jumping claims .
Ropes were procured , and but for the intercession of the
father of one and the wives and sisters of the others the
sentence would have been carried into execution . A number
of the mob made fiery speeches in favor of the execution ,
but finally the counsels of mercy prevailed . Three of the
prisoners were allowed to remain and the other four were
taken across the river into Iowa , and told if ever found
in Nebraska again , they would be hung .

More Lynching in Iowa - A Gang of Horse Thieves Broken Up -
Hanging the Ringleader . For some years past the farmers of
Clinton , Cedar and part of Scott counties , have been
greatly troubled by horse thieves . Some farmers have lost
as many as six and eight horses and others three or four ,
and so on . Several recent thefts , and knowledge that a
regular organized band of horse thieves existed in said
region , who seemed to defy all law and all efforts to
punish them , influenced the farmers of these counties to
organize a committee or society to break up this band .
Such a committee was formed a short time since , and in it
are some of the most substantial farmers in Clinton , Cedar
and Linn counties . The following facts have been given us
by a reliable informant , who is acquainted with the names
of a good many of the committee , and of certain persons
whom the committee are in search of . The facts we give are
direct from a member of the committee to our informant .
Some days ago the committee arrested two men , and placing
rope in the wagon started for the timber . This so
frightened the prisoners that they made a full confession
to save their own lives . They told all about the
organization and who were engaged in it , giving names ,
etc . , in detail . With this information the committee
proceeded to the house of a man named Page , living in the
back part of Clinton county , to arrest a horse thief who
was supposed to be concealed there . When approaching the
house , they were fired upon . They returned

following account of the murder of emigrants on their way
to California by the Cheyenne Indians . The party consisted
of T . B . Weaver and wife , A . P . Weaver , Y . Weaver ,
N . J . Lewis , Samuel Smith , J . Garrison , J . Smith ,

raised , with provisions and arms , and immediately
proceeded to the relief of the defeated party . MORE LYNCH
LAW IN IOWA—THREE MEN HUNG AND ONE SHOT . We learn through
a Clinton correspondent that Judge Lynch has been again at
work . It seems that a gang of horse thieves had been for
some time lurking in the neighborhood of the Wapsipinicon
River , in that county and their depredations had aroused
the people to vengeance . A party went in pursuit and
several of the thieves , still in possession of the stolen
horses were captured upon an island in the river . Judge
Lynch at once established his court for trial . Two were
hung on the 8th , one was hung on the 10th , and a fourth
was shot . One of those captured had disclosed the names of
the whole gang , and at the last accounts the judicial mob
were in hot pursuit of them , threatening to hang them also
. — Chicago Press . CURIOUS SEQUEL TO THE DALTON DIVORCE

not stand it apart , after all was said , and the father of
the lady has made , or is about to make , legal application
for authority to pursue and recover his daughter . The
father professes fear of personal injury to his daughter ,

and that he feared his companion was murdered . A crowd
repaired to the spot , and there lay the unfortunate man
bathed in blood , his assassins having inflicted sufficient
wounds upon his person to let out half dozen lives . Then
suspicion was excited against Bayse , who was taken into
custody and finally acknowledged that he was connected with
the two murderers , and that for some time previous they
had been implicated in a series of murders and robberies ,
one of which they had perpetrated but a short time previous
to the assassination of Stevens , and had cast the body
into the river . This confession was fully substantiated by
marks of blood found upon the river bank , as well as by
the hat and other articles of clothing which were found
belonging to the murdered man . The excitement was intense
, and the two associates of Bayse were speedily captured .
The next morning the feelings of the populace could not be
controlled . The jail was broken open , and Bayse and one

lot , the box was thrown into it , the earth rudely filled

brute , his only funeral service the curses of an excited
mob . Yet this was not the end , for the next morning early
some of his quondam executioners of the lower order ,
themselves probably fit subjects for the State prison and
the hangman , opened the grave , took the body from the box
, and placing it in wagon with clothes and hat on drove it

. In the hand of this strange boon of their revels , the

which have recently taken place in Iowa were called by

courthouse , where the prisoner was confined , and demanded
and by force took Thomas from the custody of the sheriff ,
and amid the terrifying shrieks of the prisoner and the
most hellish revelings of the mob , carried him away to a
tree which they had previously prepared , in the suburbs of
the town . Here , without even adhering to the customs of
lynch law , or abiding by the honor of mob force , they
placed the rope around the prisoner's neck , and demanded a
confession of the crime . The prisoner pleaded his
innocence . The rope was drawn over limb and the prisoner
swung into the air . After few struggles he was let down ,
and again demanded to make confession . Again he pleaded
innocent of the crime . Again he was hung up , and fire
kindled beneath his feet . The mob threatened to burn him
if he did not confess the murder . Again he was let down .
From the strangulation received he had lost his strength
and his voice . The mob still demanded confession . The
prisoner , in the faintness of death , persisted that he
was innocent . Again he was hung up , where

SUICIDE OF LYNCHER IN Iowa . - - In Cedar County , Iowa , a
young man named Finch , son of a church deacon and member
of the vigilance committee , led away by evil companions
and the counsels of men who should have set a better
example , was present when the life or death of two horse
thieves , one named Kelso , was hanging in the balance .
The lynchers took a vote on the subject , and when the
fatal hat came round , Finch dropped into it his ballot ,
inscribed with the word ' Death . ' On counting the ballots
, a majority were found to have declared that the culprits
should die , and Kelso and his comrade were accordingly
hung to a neighboring tree . Young Finch , after witnessing
their dying struggles , went home , when his mother
anxiously asked him if the thieves had been caught . ' Yes
, ' he told her , ' and hung , and he had voted to hang
them . ' His mother sadly and solemnly told him he had no
right unlawfully to take that which he could not give—the
life of a fellow being—and continued a few moments speaking
of the enormity of the act . He then left her and went out
to plough , but after ploughing a few furrows he tied his
horse , took off one of the reins , and with it hung
himself to the lower limb of a tree . When found , his
knees almost touched the ground , and he evidently could
have arisen to his feet at pleasure had he changed his
purpose before insensibility supervened . All attempts to
restore the unhappy youth to life proved useless . The
affair

Article:
OUTRAGEOUS CASE OF LYNCHING . - The Calaveras Chronicle of
Oct 10th relates the following : On Sunday evening last , a
man by the name of Lorenz was invited to ride to Mokelumne
Hill in a buggy by a man named Smith . They got in the
buggy and started towards the Hill , and when about half a
mile from Campo Seco , three men sprang from the roadside ,
stopped the horse , and took Lorenz from the buggy . Smith
then drove on with the buggy . They then took Lorenz to
high ground , put a rope round his neck , and demanded from
him some money said to have been stolen from one Hopkins .
His reply was that he knew nothing about any money
belonging to Hopkins . They then drew him up to the high
ground , and after nearly strangling him , set him down ,
and again made the same demand , but with no better success
. This was repeated three or four times , when not
receiving any confession from him , they took him to a tree
, stripped and tied him , and began whipping him-ten
strokes , and then demand for the money . This was repeated
five times ; when finding that no answer to suit them could
be had , they untied him and permitted him to go . The men
were five in number and were all masked . Lorenz had been
previously arrested on suspicion of having taken Hopkins '
money , but was discharged , without an examination , there
being no testimony against him .

since which gradually undermined his health and caused his
death . ESCAPED-On Monday night , October 9th , a Mexican
named Jesus Foutcs made his escape from the State Prison ,
and is still at large . He was serving out a sentence of
two years and half , for an assault with intent to kill ,
for which he was convicted at Shasta in September 1855 .
LYNCHED , - Four Chinese thieves were taken from a drift in

The Butte Record says these Chinamen have been in the habit
of committing various petty thefts , and until recently
have escaped detection . CALICO PARTY . - The ladies of
Michigan Bar , Amador county , are making preparations for
a calico party , to be given on the 9th of November , the
proceeds of which are to be devoted to the erection of a
school house . FIRE . - At Columbia , last week , a small
house belonging to Mr . Jones , a miner , was destroyed by
fire . Two hundred dollars in coin , which had been laid
away in some part of the premises was found among the ruins
. THE ARTESIAN WELL , at Stockton , has now a six inch
stream , with head four feet above the city grade , and it
is thought a stream of the required head will be obtained
within cut of fifty feet more . COMPLIMENTARY . - - As

on his voyage . EDITORIAL CHANGE-Mr . Mortimer J . Smith

Francisco Spirit of the Times , and Mr . Stephen T . King
assumed the duties of that position . GOING HOME . - The
officers and crew attached to the Independence will sail

LYNCHING ' PEEPING TOM ' - The Milwaukee Sentinel gives an
interesting lynching case which occurred recently at Geneva
, in that state . Several young ladies had been in the
habit of bathing in a beautiful cove formed by Geneva Lake
and nearly concealed by dense shrubbery . Ike S . , a booby
of a boy , who lives with a farmer in the capacity of help
, informed some of his mates that curiosities might be seen
there , cheaper and better than at the circus and for his
part , he would go to the show . This being reported to
some of the fair ones , they resolved to give him a lesson
. While some of the more juvenile members of the group made
apparent preparations for the bath , a committee of
full-grown females searched the thicket , and found Ike
placed very conveniently for peeping . To seize and strip
him was the work of a few moments , and the hazel brushes
which had formed his hiding place became rods of justice in
the hands of his captors , and the offender received many a
stripe for his curiosity .

LLM Explains: The article describes a specific lynching event involving

Index Number: preciv155

Article:
Sparks , who had recently settled here from Missouri ,
while on his way home , with his son and nephew , were
surrounded by a party of the Ruffians who demanded their
surrender . This they resolutely refused , and backing up
against a fence held the enemy at bay . One of the lads
returned to Easton and gave the alarm , when Mr . E . P .
Brown , of Leavenworth with fifteen men came to their
rescue . Firing immediately commenced and several were
wounded on both sides , one of the enemy mortally . Mr .
Sparks ' son received two wounds , one cutting his scalp
and the other his arm . The combatants finally separated
and both parties started for home , as was supposed . Mr .
Brown , with seven of his friends , were waylaid by a
company of thirty men on their return to Leavenworth , and
taken prisoners and carried back to Easton . They were
guarded through the day on Friday . At night , the Captain
of the band , which proved to be the Kickapoo Rangers ,
attempted to release the prisoners , and succeeded with all
excepting Brown . The company being considerably
intoxicated , would not let him go , but proceeded to make
preparations for hanging him . The Captain proposed a
compromise , and that they lynch him and let him go . This
was agreed to , when several persons sprung upon him with
hatchets and bowie knives and commenced stabbing , chopping
, beating and kicking him until he was felled to the earth
. He lay for some time , unconscious , the Ruffians
supposing him dead . As signs of life returned , they
commenced beating him again . After satisfying their
fiendish hate , they threw him into a wagon and drove him
ten miles over frozen roads , and gave him in charge to his
wife ; she , a young woman with their first child ,
received her dying husband with that agony which love and
devotion can only experience on being brutally deprived of
all the heart holds dear . In answer to inquiries , he
replied in a faint , audible voice , " I have been murdered
by a gang of cowards , in cold blood without any cause , "
and gasped and died . Thus perished one of Nature's
noblemen . He was a brave , accomplished and energetic man
; had been in Lawrence during the invasion , and was an
active member of Col . Blood's regiment of cavalry . He was
also elected a member to the Free State Legislature . His
remains were consigned to their last resting place by a few
friendly hands , on Pilot Knob , near Leavenworth , Jan .
9th , amid the howling winds and drifting snow of a dreary
Kansas winter . E . P . Brown was the son of Nose Brown ,
who was native of North Carolina and emigrated from that
State to Mississippi where he resided till 1824 . With the
view of escaping from the oppressive influence of slavery

secured . DEATH OF A MEMBER OF CONGRESS James Lockhart ,
Democratic Congressman elect from the Evansville District ,
Indiana , died at Evansville on the morning of September
9th . This makes the second member who has died since the
election in Indiana last fall . DEATH OF EX-Gov . BISSELL .
- Clark Bissell , who was chosen Governor of Connecticut in
1846 , by the Whigs , died at his residence , Norwalk ,
Conn . , , on Tuesday morning , aged 78 years . He was a
lawyer and gentleman of the old school , and very generally
respected . The OVERLAND MAIL . - The contract for carrying
the United States mail over the land route to California ,
was signed at Washington on the 10th September . Messrs .
John Butterfield , Wm . B . Dinsmore , Wm . G . Fargo ,
Alexander Holland , J . V . P . Gardner , N . L . Kenyon ,
Hamilton Spencer , and others are the contractors . Six
hundred thousand dollars per annum , for six years , is the
price to be paid the contractors . DIAMOND FOUND IN
ILLINOIS . - One of the workmen engaged in boring an
artesian well in Striker , a village on the Air Line
Railroad , about ninety miles from Toledo , found a pure
diamond recently at a depth of about 110 feet . The diamond
is of the size of an ordinary marble and competent judges
say it is worth at least $ 4,000 . Its lucky finder , poor
, honest Irishman , named Michael Shehan , no doubt
believes he ' was born to good luck ' . LYNCH LAW IN
MISSISSIPPI - The Pontotoc ( Miss . ) Examiner , of Sept .
4th contains a long account of the summary execution of
Jefferson Gray , by a large party of men , many of them it
is stated , respectable citizens . The week previous Gray
murdered Robert Abernathy , a worthy and respectable
citizen of the southern part of the county . Abernathy was
found dead in his cornfield with twelve buckshot in his
back . Suspicion fastened upon Gray , who at the time ,
with his wife and child , was living with Abernathy , and
he was forthwith apprehended . He made a full confession .
THE NEW GRANADA DIFFICULTY . - A dispatch from Washington
contains the returns of the settlement agreed upon between
the New Granada Minister and our Government . New Granada
regrets the Panama massacre , and promises to punish
offenders when convicted . A commission is to pass upon all
claims of American citizens against New Granada , from 1918
down to the Panama riot . Our Government is allowed the
privilege of purchasing an island in the Bay of Panama ,
for a coal depot . FILIBUSTERING . - The Government has
been apprised that preparations are on foot in various
parts of the country for another filibustering expedition
upon Nicaragua . It is also informed of the existence of a
scheme in Texas for the invasion of the province of Mexico
contiguous to the Rio Grande , with the design of seizing
upon Tamaulipas . Instructions have been sent from the
State Department to all the Government officers on the
Southern seaboard to stop all expeditions leaving the
United States with hostile intentions against any country
with which we are at peace . MAINE ELECTION -

Index Number: preciv153

Article:
LYNCH LAW IN SOLANO COUNTY . - - A horse belonging to the
Woliskills , in Solano County , supposed to have been
stolen , recently returned with the saddle on , to his
owners . After examining the animal , a Spaniard residing
in Vaca Valley was fixed upon as the guilty party . A
number of horsemen went to his house , and after some
conversation , he , seeing them preparing
suspicious-looking rope , put spurs to his animal and fled
. They gave chase and ran him down in the vicinity Of the
Potrero Hills . They took him to a tree and ran him up by
the neck , and would probably have let him remain there
till dead , had not one of them ascertained that they were
practicing on the wrong man .

Seco , says the Calaveras Chronicle , have been committing

A CRUEL LYNCHING CASE-FOUR HUNDRED LASHES TO A MAN'S FACE .
- A shocking exhibition of Lynch law took place at
Bushville , Sullivan county , New York , on Thursday , Nov
. 5th , upon a man who has the reputation of doing many of
the evil acts perpetrated in that neighborhood . A day or
two previous , a cow belonging to an individual with whom
Terpenning had quarreled was maimed , and suspicion at once
rested on the scapegoat of the neighborhood . About 10
o'clock , on Thursday morning , Nov . 5th , eleven men
broke into Terpenning's house , tied a rope around his neck
, and dragged him from his bed . They then tied his hands
behind him , took him to a secluded place about a mile from
his home and commenced whipping him with horse whips . They
told him that they would lash him until he acknowledged
that he had cut the cow , but he denied having done so .
They then tied his feet and took turns at whipping ,
occasionally resting to ask him to confess but he continued
to protest his innocence until about 6 o'clock , when , he
says , fearing that they would whip him to death , and they
swearing that they would do so if he did not own it , he
acknowledged that he had maimed the animal . He was then
taken to the house of one of the party , and as he alleges
, his body exposed in an indecent manner . Terpenning's
wife followed the party and was close by all the while they
were maltreating her husband . She heard him cry and plead
with them not to kill him but dared not go to his

Headline: 
Newspaper: New-York daily tribune.
Date: 1858-02-02
Article ID: 7_1858-02-02_p3_sn83030213_00206530571_1858020201_0211
LLM Explains: The article describes a specific event where citizens
detained criminals under threat of lynching ('threat of
hanging'), which aligns with historical patterns of
extrajudicial measure resembling lynching despite the
ultimate decision to spare lives.
Index Number: preciv148

Article:
Counterfeiters and Horse-Thieves Regulated The citizens of
Noble County , having become wearied by the depredations
and rascalities committed by the band of outlaws who have
long infested that county , have risen to the number of
several hundreds with the determination to rid themselves
of the scoundrels . They have arrested several of the most
notorious offenders , and keep them in custody . One named
Malcolm Burnham or Barnum , a wholesale counterfeit dealer
was induced , under threat of hanging to make full
confession and gave up the names of his accomplices in that
and adjoining counties . Twenty-eight of the accused are
now in the hands of the Regulators , twenty at Ligonier and
eight at Albion . Serious thoughts were at one time
entertained of executing summary punishment on the
offenders but vote being taken at a meeting of several
hundred citizens , a majority being opposed to proceeding
to such an extremity , their lives spared and they will be
handed to the civil power , where it is to be hoped they
will meet their deserts . Much as we deprecate such violent
proceedings , and are opposed to superseding the legal
tribunals by Judge Lynch's speedy and severe code , we must
admit that there are many circumstances to justify the
people of Noble County . They have long been cursed by the
presence of a numerous and well-organized gang of
scoundrels , who have set the laws at defiance and escape
with impunity . It has been almost impossible to convict
any who might be arrested and to break up the gang by
lawful means was impracticable . The citizens have at
length taken the matter into their own hands , and , we
hope , will make thorough work and rout out the entire gang
. The prosperity as well as the reputation of the county
demands it . A lawyer who went from this city to endeavor
to obtain the Liberation of the accused was looked upon as
an accomplice or at least as an aider and abettor of the
scoundrels , and was summarily arrested and confined with
his supposed confederates . After being held in durance two
or three days , he was liberated and lodged back at speed ,
wiser and better man , glad to find his neck unstretched
even though minus his expected fee . If this method of
treating all who give aid and comfort to the rascals is
rigidly carried out , we have strong hopes that the gang
will be thoroughly routed and Noble County be eventually
cleared of those who have been so long a terror and
annoyance to its good citizens .

Article:
Lynch Law at Monterey - Anastasia Jesus , sentenced to
death for the crime of murder , at Monterey , but respited
by Gov . Weller , was hung by the citizens of that place
Feb 18th , the day on which the respite of the Governor
came into the hands of the Sheriff . The people were much
incensed at the interference of the Executive in behalf of
a murderer beyond dispute , and took the execution of the
laws in their own hands . This has been done in the face of
the bombastic threats of Gov . Weller's inaugural and the
terrors of Mr . Lee's vigilance bill . Gov . Weller will
find an uphill job before him if he attempts the punishment
of the citizens of Monterey for seeing the laws executed in
spite of the prerogatives of himself . We shall now see the
fruits of idle threats .

Article:
HUNG BY A MOB A negro named Bracy , on Friday , 10th inst ,
at Auburn , violently assaulted a miner named Murphy , by
striking the sharp point of a pick into the back part of
his head , through which the brain protruded . The friends
of Murphy assembled and demanded the person of the negro ,
which was refused by the Sheriff , whereupon the jail was
broken open and the negro taken out and hung . Much
excitement prevails , and great efforts are now being made
to bring these ' lynchers ' to a sense of duty to their
country . Certain law and order journals are howling , for
buncombe , and trying to excite ill-feelings and raise a
breeze in order to blow their pet ' mob-bill ' out of the
reach of ignominious defeat , by appealing to the case .
The negro , no doubt , thought he had as good a right to
take life with impunity , as had Herbert , but then he
forgot that he was not a Member of Congress and was in
California , where some things can be done as well as
others . The Auburn Press thus alludes to the case and
takes off the Supreme Court in a parody of ' their opinion
on the Archy case ' : We cannot justify or excuse the mob
that hung Bracy , except on the ground of the late decision
of the Supreme Court in the ' Archy case . ' These people
were very much excited , many were young and
inexperienced-others , perhaps , in poor health-and it was
the first violation of the law . If their case should be
brought before the next grand jury , they would be forced
to notice it-and , in their report , they could say : '
This is a fragrant violation of law and the Constitution ,
but as the parties are young men , inexperienced , in poor
health , and it being the first case in which the county
jail has been broken down , hitherto supposed almost
impregnable ; and with the fullest confidence the like will
never be attempted again , we find them guilty , but demand
no indictments . '

hanging by Lynch law , at Rogue River Valley , Oregon , of

. Geysel was a married man and was the father of three

Article:
THE NEWS OF THE FORTNIGHT . From Our Own Correspondent SAN
FRANCISCO , Feb . 20 , 1853 The public mind of the State
has just been startled by an appeal to Lynch-law in Placer
County . Some weeks ago Mr . Murphy bought from a negro
named Bracy a piece of ground near Auburn , the county seat
of Placer . The day before yesterday , at 3 o'clock ,
Murphy went to the ground and found Bracy fencing it ; the
former demonstrated , and was turning to go away when Bracy
struck him in the back of the head with a pick , driving
the point three or four inches into the brain and
inflicting a mortal wound . Such is reported to be Bracy's
statement for so far as it appears there was no witness to
the tragedy . Immediately after the affair Bracy went into
town , told what he had done to the Sheriff , gave himself
up , and was imprisoned . At 1 o'clock yesterday morning ,
a mob seized the Sheriff and his deputies , broke the jail
doors open with sledgehammers , took Bracy out to a tree ,
and hung him . The rope was not put on properly , and the
knot came just under his chin , so that though very
uncomfortable it did not interfere with his breathing . He
hung about a minute , but could not stand it any longer ,
and cried out ' Lord God gentlemen ! I can't die this way .
' He was then set down , and the knot placed where it
belonged . When he was again hoisted up , and soon passed
away for Bracy . It is said that Bracy killed a Chinaman
near the same ground a year ago , when there was no witness
to the transaction and was acquitted on his own statement
of the occurrence . Such a tragedy would cause an
excitement at

WRONGLY SUSPECTED . - The Amador since buried his purse of
gold near his cabin , and subsequently , failing to find it
, suspected a certain person living nearby , who in
consequence , narrowly escaped lynching . A few days ago ,
however , the purse owner repaired to the ground where he
made his cache , and by digging , found his lost treasure .
Thus , he saved his gold , and the suspected person saved
his character . The latter has gone to the Atlantic States
.

Quite a number of cases have been tried before this popular
tribunal lately , in various parts of the Territory . Many
of our citizens are aware to their cost , of the existence
of horse-thieving combinations . Robberies of this kind
have been very numerous , and the sufferers have become
exasperated as a consequence . At Leroy , on Thursday , 31
inst , two brothers named Claywell were arrested , with
several stolen horses in their possession , and one of them
, Walter Claywell , was hung by order of Judge Lynch . At
Burlingame , on Monday , 31 ult . , at the time our
informant left , two men named Hysop or Hyslop , were being
tried by the citizens for various offences , one of them
having robbed and shot a young lad on the Santa Fe road a
few days before . This one would probably be hung . A Lynch
trial was going on at Hyat for horse stealing on Monday ,
7th inst . The same results are anticipated in that case .
A young man named Anderson was arrested on Wednesday
afternoon , at Pierce's , near Lawrence , on suspicion of
being connected with the lately discovered horse-thieving
gang . He was placed in the Lawrence jail for safe keeping
. We are pained at such state of things as would seem in
the least degree to justify a resort to such extreme
measures yet it is a notorious fact that property is
becoming unsafe , and measures must be taken to stop the
depredations . We however trust the extreme penalty of
death will not be resorted to in such cases .

MURDER AND LYNCH LAW Letter from Lexington , in the
Louisville Democrat , gives the particulars of a tragedy
which transpired in that place on the 10th inst . : ' Mr .
Joseph Beard , the City Marshal , gentleman esteemed , and
deservedly so , by all parties , was in the market this
morning , in the discharge of his duties , when a man by
the name of William Barker , a notorious ruffian , bully ,
and thief , forced Mr . John McChesney into a difficulty .
Mr . Beard interfered , and told Barker that he must go to
the watch-house . Barker walked off some twenty paces with
him , and then drawing a small bowie knife , stabbed him in
the side , throwing him off the curb . As he struggled to
rise , his murderer took his hand and assisted him partly
to his feet , and then struck him again in the side with
the knife , turning it around in the wound , until it made
a gash into which a man's fist might be thrust . Barker was
immediately arrested and conveyed to jail . In the course
of half an hour the city was in a ferment . At about 2
o'clock , two hours and half after the murder , the court
house bell and all the fire bells in the city rang ; this
seemed to be the signal . In an incredibly short space of
time the court house and yard were filled with people . A
motion was made and seconded to hang the murderer at once .
The response was a rush to the jail . The jailor , Mr .

From The Lafayette Old Conductor 7th . A man named Robert
Coons , married , and respectably connected and who , it
will be remembered , obtained very unenviable notoriety
last Spring , by running away with another man's wife , was
tarred and feathered at Charlottesville last night . It
seems that the gay Lothario has been in correspondence for
some months past with a respectable young lady of that town
, and has proposed an elopement , to which she consented .
By arrangement , the deluded girl was to go to the depot on
the evening they were to leave together on the first train
for the South . Accordingly , on Monday , she went down to
the depot , and was followed yesterday morning by her gay
deceiver . In the meantime , the people of Crawfordsville
had got wind of the affair , and a meeting of fifty or more
citizens was held , and a committee of five resolute men
appointed with instructions to proceed at once to the depot
and take Coons in custody . At the depot , they captured
him and brought him back on the 10 o'clock train last night
. They were met at the depot by a large crowd of citizens ,
and the guilty wretch was escorted to the Court-House ,
which had been lighted up for the occasion . Judge Lynch
organized his Court , and Mr . Coons was put upon his trial
. Eighteen love letters in his signature , and addressed to
the young lady whom he had sought to victimize , were
produced and read to the multitude . No other evidence was
needed , and after a few speeches had been made by
prominent citizens , the lights were suddenly extinguished
, Coons caught and dragged to the Court-House yard , every
stitch of clothing torn from his back , a bucket of warm
tar poured over his head , and a bag of feathers
artistically applied . A more hideous looking object , says
our informant , cannot be imagined . He was coated from
head to heels . The committee took him in charge and
escorted him to the outskirts of the town , where they
humanely provided him with a suit of clothing and , by a
vigorous application of soft soap and lard , removed the
tar and feathers . He was placed aboard the train for this
city today and leaves by the Valley road tonight for
California . From The St . Joseph Mo . Gazette July 8 The
Journal of yesterday says a gentleman who arrived in this

informs us that on Monday evening last , James Milligan ,

which made the act , in the estimation of the people , a
cruel outrage . The murderer was arrested and put in Jail
at Albany and word sent to Judge Norton , at Platte city ,
that if a special term was not held immediately for the
trial of the prisoner , he would be tried before Judge
Lynch . Judge Norton called the special term as requested ;
but , on the trial , it was found that two of the
prisoner's important witnesses had been driven off and
could not be found . The counsel for the defense asked a
continuance which was granted ; and the Judge , inferring
from the appearance of things that an attempt would be made
by the crowd to take the prisoner out of the hands of the
officers , appointed a guard of twenty five men to conduct
him to jail . But the guard had no sooner taken its
position than a rush was made by the crowd , and the
prisoner torn from the officers and borne off to the woods
a few hundred yards distant . Arrived there , the prisoner
was allowed to say his prayers , after which a rope was put
around his neck and he was hung to trees . The mob was
composed of about fifty persons . - - St . Louis News .

fights have occurred between the two parties , at which the

. Pearce complained bitterly of the losses which the
slaveholders suffer . Of late years the slaves have escaped
in gangs , as many as ten or twelve going off at once ,
taking with them the best horses and carriages of their
owners , and under circumstances which showed a
preconcerted plan . Bowers was suspected and believed to
have aided and abetted in these operations , and since no
legal remedy could be had against him , some respectable
citizens of ‘ the county ’ took it upon themselves to drive
him out of the state . Mr . Pearce seemed quite astonished
that anybody should object to this method of procedure or
should undertake to apply a similar process of lynch law to
those concerned in it . Either this spirit must be crushed
, or conservative people must sell their lands to Northern
folks for what they could get , and seek homes elsewhere .
A series of resolutions was passed justifying the outrage
upon Bowers , and pledging the persons assembled to sustain
the operators against punishment or violence . It remains ,
however , to be seen whether the non-slaveholders will be
thus cowed . The Courier and Enquirer grievously mistakes .

Lynch Law in Texas . Last fall a preacher named Donegan
received five hundred lashes from leather strap
administered in presence of large audience of the citizens
of Ellis County , Texas because the citizens of Ellis
believed him guilty of arson burglary , horse-stealing ,
and tampering with negroes . He was also banished from the
county . On the 10th of back in the county ; that he was
coming to Waxahachie , and intended bringing friends with
him to defend himself against opposition . In confirmation
of the report , two of Donegan's friends , named John W .
Bushby , and Donegan's brother , suddenly appeared
well-armed , and saying that Donegan should come next day
at all hazards . The citizens of Waxahachie , upon this ,
became highly excited , and as soon as the rose , men armed
and mounted were seen riding in small squads on . Of town
all went in the direction of the Donegan and Bushby
neighborhood . It was estimated that near one hundred
persons thus armed and equipped left town that morning .
What they intended doing was mystery . Before night ,
however , their design was manifest . They had gone in
pursuit of and killed both Bushby and John Donegan . Bushby
was shot in the head , arms , breast and side , and had
more than a dozen mortal wounds . Donegan was also riddled
with nine balls . Texas Paper .

Index Number: preciv123

Article:
MORE LYNCH LAW IN MISSOURI It was stated recently that a
man named Kessler had been hung by a mob in Gentry County ,
Mo . , for killing another named Timmons . An accomplice
named Milligan was arrested at the time but his case was
laid over until the next term of court . A letter from St .
Joseph , July 9th says ; a guard was appointed to watch his
prison . No appropriation was made for their support , and
they became wearied with such thankless and tedious an
employment , and abandoned their posts . The mob went to
the county court yesterday and demanded an appropriation
for the expenses of guard , and , upon a refusal , they
proceeded to the prison , took Milligan out and led him to
the same tree upon which Kessler had ten days before been
hung . He requested to be baptized , which was done , in a
brook near by . Here he also had an interview with his old
father , which was deeply affecting . He was then taken
back to the tree and hung until dead .

covered in Yolo county , about three miles from Cache Creek

Horrible Murder and Lynching . - A most brutal murder was
committed on Monday afternoon , Oct . 18th , at Cooks Bar ,
Sacramento county , by a Chinaman . The Chinaman entered
the store of Mrs . Sarah Neal , while she was the only
occupant , and stabbed her three times with a knife ,
inflicting a mortal wound each time . She died instantly .
The murderer was taken by the people of the Bar , hastily
tried , and hung to a tree . It is supposed that his object
was robbery . Mrs . Neal's husband had gone to Fraser River
, and she was keeping a provision store on her own account
. Sickness at Oroville . - A gentleman recently from
Oroville , states that the people there and in the
surrounding country are still suffering from the unusual
prevalence of febrile diseases that have appeared there
during the past summer . There has never , we believe ,
been so much sickness in any part of the country as there
has been during this season in that region of the country ;
but the disease is , and has been of a type more
disagreeable than dangerous , fatal cases being extremely
rare . Death of Patriarch . - The Los Angeles Star notices
the death of Don Antonio Ignacio Abila , at that place , on
the 25th ult . His age was not known , but he is supposed
to have been over ninety years old . He was born in the
Villa de Fuerte , Mexico , and came when very young to
California ; served his country as a soldier , and by
industry and integrity accumulated great wealth-the greater
part of which he gave away in works of charity .

Article:
the jail , which is built of stone and very strong , and
found the doors locked and the keeper gone . A number of
men immediately seized large pieces of heavy timber , and ,
with several tremendous blows , burst the door from its
hinges . An inner door was speedily broken with crowbars
and sledgehammers , and a portion of the mob stood in the
debtors ' room fronting the cells in which the miserable
men were confined . The locks of the cell doors were soon
broken and the doors pried open , when most horrid and
appalling scenes were present . One of the men , Elias
Scaggs , a man weighing two hundred pounds , was found
weltering in a large pool of blood in the last agonies of
death , the blood spurting in large jets from a ghastly and
self-inflicted wound in his neck , having with a razor cut
his throat from ear to ear . His body , convulsed in death
, was dragged into the debtors ' room and from thence down
a flight of steps onto the street paving . The remaining
three men , viz : Bill Saul Thompson , Sloan Despano and
George Hunter , were then brought from the jail bound and
mounted behind men on horseback , when the crowd , in
double file , and throngs of spectators took up their
return march to this place , a distance of twelve miles ,
during which two of the miserable men displayed
considerable firmness , asserting their innocence in the
most positive terms . Thompson wept and prayed aloud by a
good part of the way , calling on all to witness his
innocence . The crowd having arrived at this place
determined to lynch Beko also , a negro belonging to the
murdered man , who had been implicated by Scaggs as the
real murderer . At this juncture , amid the yells and
howlings of the crowd , the expostulations of the Sheriff
and our excellent tailor , amid confusion worse confounded
, Robert Colvin , Esquire , a prominent and influential
citizen , having succeeded in gaining an elevated position
and in catching the ear of the crowd , addressed it in a
short speech which , for apt appropriateness and good sense
, I have hardly ever heard excelled , and it was as
effectual as it was sensible . After an unsuccessful effort
of two inexperienced orators , the mob left the negro in
jail and conducted the three a short distance to an elm
tree , and began making preparations to hang them . At
Thompson's request , prayer was offered in their behalf ,
he praying and weeping aloud during the time . He was then
mounted on a horse , and after asserting his innocence for
the last time , a rope was put about his neck , the end
thrown over a limb , the horse driven from under , and
Thompson was launched into eternity . He seemed to die
easily—one or two spasmodic jerkings of the limbs , a
shudder , and all was over . Despano soon shared a similar
fate , dying much harder . At this stage , Hunter gave
evidence of making a clean breast , which , after some
delay and a good deal of reluctance , he did , confessing
to the guilt of Scaggs , Thompson , and Hunter , and
implicating five others as being concerned in the murder .

Of the Southern Overland Mail . SAN FRANCISCO , Nov . 23rd
, 1858 . The overland mail stage arrived at half-past seven

hundred Rangers to scout the Indians on the northern
frontier . They will cooperate with Major Van Dorn's
Cavalry . The news from the Western Kansas gold mines is

by Kentucky policemen and taken across the river by
intimidation of the ferrymen . When the people of New
Albany , who had been attending fair , heard it , they rung
the city bells , had the ferrymen arrested as kidnappers ,
seized the ferry boat , proposed rescuing them from
Brandenburg , and at last accounts had broken into the
arsenal to procure arms for that purpose . The Philadelphia
police are on the lookout for French woman , Emile De Gazin
, and a man , who have been swindling through the Southern
States and California , by raising money on drafts .
William Franklin , lumber merchant of San Francisco , was
victimized five thousand dollars . Luella Kentry , daughter

from eating arsenic in mistake for cream of tartar . Gen .
Finley , of Patterson , has been put forward by the people
of Kansas for Governor . George Dunson was hung in Uvalde
County , Texas , by lynch law , for horse stealing . There
is much excitement in the east about the prize fight .
Heenan has challenged Morrissey to another fight for $
4,000 side or upwards to take place in four or six months .
Morrissey is giving sparring exhibitions in Buffalo . Tom
Hyer , to make up for losses on Heenan , challenges
Morrissey , or the world , to fight for $ 40,000 side .

on Saturday evening week , and inquired for Jane Norris , a

left in pursuit , when Miss Norris made her escape , and
though much bruised and fatigued , managed to get back to
New Castle . Turner and Robinson returned the horse and
carriage to Mr . McBride , to whom they belonged , about
three o'clock on Sunday morning , leaving the dirk behind
them . When the news of the outrage reached the village ,
great indignation was felt , and if they could have found
the villains , they would probably have been lynched .

was conveyed to New Castle jail by High Constable Hawkins .
Turner , going here by the cognomen of Charlie , was known
to many of our citizens , having worked in Smyrna several
months two years since . He was then considered a desperate
fellow . It is to be hoped that he may yet be arrested and
made to suffer what he deserves—the highest penalty
inflicted by our statute laws . The eighth of December has
been fixed by the Court for the trial of Alexander Robinson
, arrested for rape on Jane Norris . Turner is yet at large
. Miss Norris was so much injured that on Wednesday last
her recovery was considered doubtful . On Friday , however
, her system rallied , and she was able to answer
interrogatories addressed to her . Robinson , it is said ,
pretends to regret his participation in this outrage , and
says if he gets out of this scrape safely , he will never
be caught in another .

No Lynch Law In Kentucky . We understand that for several
days past , the most intense excitement has prevailed in
Larue County , in consequence of the confession of a
startling tragedy . John , a slave , the property of Berry
Cundiff , was arrested about one year ago charged with the
murder of Miss Mary Shaler , has confessed his
participation in the crime and implicates several other
persons . This Miss Shaler was originally a resident of
Bardstown but at the time of the murder temporarily living
in Larue County , with Mrs . Murphy . The case has been
twice tried , but each time jurors failed to agree . A few
days since , the negro was induced to make a clean breast
of the horrible affair being threatened with lynching ;
persons telling him that the Taylor County mob was coming
to hang him . He says that four other persons were
connected with him in the affair , he being a mere tool and
receiving $ 100 . The persons charged are Mrs . Murphy ,
her daughter , aged sixteen years , James McGavock , and
William Pickrell . These parties were all immediately
arrested . Great excitement existed throughout the
community and exasperated were the citizens that it was
with much difficulty the officers could prevent the crowd
from lynching the accused . In fact , Pickrell was at one
time strung up . The circumstances that led to the murder
are said to be owing to the fact that Miss Shaler was
supposed to have a considerable sum of money . It is
sincerely believed however that the true cause was that
Pickrell's brother was about to marry the unfortunate young
woman , concerning whose virtue there were various reports
.

Headline: Lynch Law.
Newspaper: The weekly Butte record.
Date: 1858-12-11
Article ID: 72_1858-12-11_p1_sn86058108_00279557359_1858121101_0374
LLM Explains: The article specifically describes two lynching events
involving Harrison Morgan and Pancho Daniel, where they
were taken by mobs and hanged. Keywords and terms like
'mob', 'taken from the hands of the authorities', 'hanged',
and 'lynch' support this categorization.
Index Number: preciv113

Article:
A few days since , a man named Harrison Morgan , the
murderer of officer John Leary , of Columbia , Tuolumne
county , was taken from the hands of the authorities by a
mob , and after a brief trial was hanged . Leary , our
readers will remember , was shot while attempting to arrest
Morgan and some other thieves . On November 30th , Pancho
Daniel , one of the murderers of Sheriff Barton of Los
Angeles , was taken from the jail of that town and hung ;
the mob meeting with little or no resistance . These two
men most unquestionably deserved hanging , but that fact
should have been demonstrated by fair legal trial . These
rash acts of maddened people seem to be as much in vogue as
they were in earlier days when the courts of justice were
too often but places of refuge for guilty criminals and the
more frequently they occur , the more difficult does it
become to repress their re-enactment . They should never
occur without bringing upon their perpetrators the utmost
vigor of the law's provisions , and we should be glad to
see Governor Weller act with as much energy and promptness
toward these followers of the Code of Judge Lynch as he
promised to do in his inaugural . We believe that
infinitely less harm is done by the escape from punishment
through the technicalities of law , of criminals , than is
wrought by the setting aside of law and the functions of
the Judiciary by infuriated mobs .

has disappeared from Charleston . The Mercury SAYS ' Since
the last two days of grey wintry skies , with cold and
bracing atmosphere , all apprehensions of yellow fever for
friends coming to Charleston have disappeared Fires are
found in every house , and the most eminent physicians have
given their unqualified professional opinions that it is
perfectly safe for all to come , whether from the North
East , South or West-from the mountain or the sea . '

Article:
ADMINISTRATION OF JUSTICE IN ARKANSAS - A friend who has
recently traveled through a portion of the State of
Arkansas , and especially through Red River Bottom where
the following incident occurred related it in our editorial
sanctum as follows : A man by the name of Rogers stole a
horse from an honest old farmer of Arkansas , and wended
his way up into Washington County where he said he sold it
to a farmer there . But upon his not being able to give the
other man , nor make satisfactory explanation , a company
who had been in pursuit of the horse and thief took him
into the woods and tied him to a log and gave him between
two hundred and three hundred lashes well laid on . This
had the effect of bringing him to terms . He then confessed
where the horse was , and who were his accessories . The
company then proceeded to Red River Bottom , where they
found the missing horse and five fellows , whose business
it was to steal all the horses , and whatever else they
could , whom they hung to limbs of trees until they were
dead , dead , dead . Upon consideration of Rogers having
turned , he was spared . As our informant rode past the
spot where the edict of lynch law had gone forth , and the
horrible spectacle of its execution was witnessed , one
poor fellow was seen dangling between heaven and earth
suspended by his neck to the limb of a tree , whose
branches extended across the road with this inscription
written upon his back : ' Tennessee horse thief , if you
like him better than I do , you can take him down and bury
him . ' We will simply state that our informant is a highly
respectable gentleman , and this narrative may be relied
upon as true . Memphis Eagle . This man Rogers is , we
believe , the same for whose misdeeds one Thos . J . Rodden
was severely handled by the lynchers , through mistake .
